25	1. Descriptor queue endpoints: paired DescSender (loan +
26	   send) / DescReceiver (recv) [[11]]:
27	   - own ring endpoint + registry access;
28	   - the demo's ~20-line send path becomes ~3 lines;
29	   - `resolve`'s unsafe is audited once inside the crate
30	     (recv safe by construction);
31	   - guard handed back on Full;
32	   - design against both ring flavors (SPSC + MPSC);
33	   - the sender is also where each sender's private
34	     overflow pending list will live.
35	2. Overflow FIFO: on ring Full, append the message to a
36	   sender-private pending list instead of failing
37	   [details](ring-buffer-design.md#overflow-fifo-future):
38	   - intrusive — the same embedded next-link the
39	     free-stack uses, so zero allocation;
40	   - naturally bounded by pool capacity;
41	   - composes per-sender with MPSC — see
42	     [Overflow readiness](ring-buffer-design.md#overflow-readiness).
43	3. Explore why zcr-mpsc-2t measures faster than zcr-with-2t
44	   (73.9 vs 100.1 ns adjusted mean at 300s, 1p/1c) and
45	   whether the mechanism can improve SPSC [[20]]:
46	   - we think SPSC bounces two index lines per handoff
47	     (each side polls the line the other writes) while
48	     MPSC's only shared hot word is the slot seq;
49	   - verify with perf cache-transfer counters and/or the
50	     padded-seq variant (a design open question);
51	   - if confirmed, a seam-word variant might feed back
52	     into the SPSC protocol.
53	4. Batch alloc/free demo: alongside the one-message
54	   alloc_free_1t loops, a variant that allocs X messages
55	   (5, 10, …) then frees them all, pool vs global
56	   allocator. We think the pool's rate stays constant
57	   (pop/push is O(1) regardless of live count, LIFO keeps
58	   the working set hot) while Box::new/drop slows as the
59	   batch outgrows malloc's thread-cache fast path — the
60	   demo should show it.

1	//! MPSC ring: multi-producer single-consumer sibling of the
2	//! SPSC ring, per the design doc's "MPSC ring (sibling
3	//! primitive)" section:
4	//!
5	//! - Same region shape as the SPSC ring — a four-line
6	//!   [`MpscHeader`] then slots — plus a per-slot sequence
7	//!   array between them: `seq[pos]` publishes each slot
8	//!   independently, because claim order and commit order
9	//!   differ under concurrent producers.
10	//! - Producers CAS-claim `producer_idx`; the consumer stays
11	//!   CAS-free. Fullness is read from the slot seq, so
12	//!   producers never load `consumer_idx`.
13	//! - Own magic and layout version: a region is one kind or
14	//!   the other, and cross-attaching fails toward
15	//!   [`Error::BadMagic`].
16	//! - The claim CAS raises the atomic floor for MPSC users
17	//!   only — this module is gated on `target_has_atomic =
18	//!   "32"`; the SPSC ring stays load/store-only.
19	
20	use core::marker::PhantomData;
21	use core::mem::size_of;
22	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
23	
24	use crate::{CACHE_LINE_SIZE, CacheAligned, Error, USER_WORDS};
25	
26	mod consumer;
27	mod producer;
28	
29	pub use consumer::{MpscConsumer, MpscReadSlot};
30	pub use producer::MpscProducer;
31	
32	/// Layout marker written by [`MpscRing::init`]; distinct from
33	/// the SPSC magic so cross-kind attach fails toward
34	/// [`Error::BadMagic`].
35	const MPSC_MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_4D31; // "ZCM1"
36	
37	/// Bumped on any change to the MPSC region layout;
38	/// independent of the SPSC layout version.
39	const MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 1;
40	
41	/// Capacity bound: `2^30`, not the SPSC `2^31` — the seq
42	/// tombstone encoding reserves index-arithmetic headroom (see
43	/// the design doc's "MPSC open questions").
44	const MPSC_MAX_CAPACITY: u32 = 1 << 30;
45	
46	/// Tombstone offset: an unwound `send_with` commits
47	/// `pos + 1 + TOMBSTONE` instead of `pos + 1`, marking a
48	/// claimed slot the consumer must release without delivering.
49	///
50	/// - Unambiguous at both wait points: the consumer at `c`
51	///   legitimately sees only `seq - (c+1)` of `-1` (not yet
52	///   committed) or `0` (committed), so exactly `2^31` means
53	///   tombstone; a producer's wrapping-signed diff reads a
54	///   tombstoned previous lap as negative — "full" — until the
55	///   consumer skips it, which is the correct backpressure.
56	pub(crate) const TOMBSTONE: u32 = 1 << 31;
57	
58	/// Control block at offset 0 of an MPSC region — same
59	/// four-line shape as the SPSC [`Header`](crate::Header), but
60	/// its own type: the layouts evolve independently and the
61	/// index-ownership story differs.
62	///
63	/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`MpscRing::init`] with
64	///   `magic` last (`Release`), read-only thereafter.
65	/// - line 1: `producer_idx` — CAS-claimed by *all* producers
66	///   (the contended line), never read by the consumer's hot
67	///   path.
68	/// - line 2: `consumer_idx` — written only by the consumer;
69	///   never read by producers (fullness comes from the slot
70	///   seq), so it is resume state and diagnostic occupancy.
71	/// - line 3: `user`, app-owned scratch — same contract as the
72	///   SPSC user line.
73	/// - Every field is atomic: the region may be mapped by a
74	///   peer at any time.
75	#[repr(C)]
76	pub struct MpscHeader {
77	    /// Layout marker ([`MPSC_MAGIC`]); stored last by init
78	    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`).
79	    magic: AtomicU32,
80	    /// Layout version ([`MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION`]).
81	    layout_version: AtomicU32,
82	    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.
83	    slot_size: AtomicU32,
84	    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^30`.
85	    capacity: AtomicU32,
86	    /// [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] this region was built with.
87	    cache_line_size: AtomicU32,
88	    /// Free-running count of slot positions claimed by
89	    /// producers (CAS).
90	    producer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
91	    /// Free-running count of messages released; consumer-owned
92	    /// resume state, not read by producers.
93	    consumer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
94	    /// App-owned scratch line ([`USER_WORDS`] words): zeroed by
95	    /// init, then never touched by the crate.
96	    user: CacheAligned<[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS]>,
97	}
98	
99	const _: () = assert!(size_of::<MpscHeader>() == 4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
100	
101	/// A validated view over an MPSC ring region.
102	///
103	/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at init/attach,
104	///   as the SPSC ring does: per-op paths never re-read fields
105	///   a peer could scribble on.
106	/// - Region layout: [`MpscHeader`], the seq array (`M ×
107	///   AtomicU32`, padded up to a cache line), then M slots of N
108	///   bytes.
109	pub struct MpscRing<'a> {
110	    /// The region's control block.
111	    header: &'a MpscHeader,
112	    /// Base of the per-slot sequence array.
113	    seqs: *const AtomicU32,
114	    /// Base of the slot array.
115	    slots: *mut u8,
116	    /// Snapshot of `header.slot_size`.
117	    slot_size: u32,
118	    /// Snapshot of `header.capacity`.
119	    capacity: u32,
120	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
121	    mask: u32,
122	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
123	}
124	
125	impl<'a> MpscRing<'a> {
126	    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.
127	    ///
128	    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
129	    ///   multiple.
130	    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^30`.
131	    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned and at
132	    ///   least [`mpsc_region_size`] bytes.
133	    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {
134	        validate_mpsc_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
135	        let len = region.len();
136	        // Taken exactly once — see `Ring::init` for the Stacked
137	        // Borrows rationale.
138	        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();
139	        let header = mpsc_header_ptr(base, len)?;
140	        if (len as u64) < mpsc_region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
141	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
142	        }
143	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the MpscHeader checked
144	        // by mpsc_header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed
145	        // for 'a; any byte pattern is a valid MpscHeader
146	        // (all-atomic fields, plain-byte padding).
147	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
148	        header
149	            .layout_version
150	            .store(MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);
151	        header.slot_size.store(slot_size, Ordering::Relaxed);
152	        header.capacity.store(capacity, Ordering::Relaxed);
153	        header
154	            .cache_line_size
155	            .store(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);
156	        header.producer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
157	        header.consumer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
158	        for word in header.user.iter() {
159	            word.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
160	        }
161	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len covers header + seq array.
162	        let seqs = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<MpscHeader>()) } as *const AtomicU32;
163	        // seq[i] = i marks every slot claimable by the position
164	        // that will first reach it.
165	        for i in 0..capacity {
166	            // SAFETY: i < capacity, inside the seq array
167	            // validated by the region-size check.
168	            unsafe { &*seqs.add(i as usize) }.store(i, Ordering::Relaxed);
169	        }
170	        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region must
171	        // never observe the magic before the geometry and seq
172	        // stores it validates and relies on.
173	        header.magic.store(MPSC_MAGIC, Ordering::Release);
174	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len covers header + seqs + slots.
175	        let slots = unsafe { base.add(slots_offset(capacity)) };
176	        Ok(MpscRing {
177	            header,
178	            seqs,
179	            slots,
180	            slot_size,
181	            capacity,
182	            mask: capacity - 1,
183	            _region: PhantomData,
184	        })
185	    }
186	
187	    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)
188	    /// already initialized, validating its header.
189	    ///
190	    /// # Safety
191	    ///
192	    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive
193	    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a
194	    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).
195	    /// - At most one consumer attaches; any number of
196	    ///   producers may (MPSC contract).
197	    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {
198	        let header = mpsc_header_ptr(region, len)?;
199	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the MpscHeader checked
200	        // by mpsc_header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is
201	        // live and shared.
202	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
203	        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic: a
204	        // valid magic means the geometry and seq stores are
205	        // visible.
206	        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != MPSC_MAGIC {
207	            return Err(Error::BadMagic);
208	        }
209	        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION {
210	            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);
211	        }
212	        if header.cache_line_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32 {
213	            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);
214	        }
215	        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read it.
216	        let slot_size = header.slot_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
217	        let capacity = header.capacity.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
218	        validate_mpsc_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
219	        if (len as u64) < mpsc_region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
220	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
221	        }
222	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len covers header + seqs + slots.
223	        let seqs = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<MpscHeader>()) } as *const AtomicU32;
224	        // SAFETY: as above.
225	        let slots = unsafe { region.add(slots_offset(capacity)) };
226	        Ok(MpscRing {
227	            header,
228	            seqs,
229	            slots,
230	            slot_size,
231	            capacity,
232	            mask: capacity - 1,
233	            _region: PhantomData,
234	        })
235	    }
236	
237	    /// Split into one producer handle and the consumer handle.
238	    ///
239	    /// - The producer is `Clone`: one clone per producing
240	    ///   thread (MPSC contract). The consumer is unique per
241	    ///   process; cross-process, exactly one consuming process
242	    ///   is the caller's contract (see [`MpscRing::attach`]).
243	    pub fn split(self) -> (MpscProducer<'a>, MpscConsumer<'a>) {
244	        (
245	            MpscProducer::new(
246	                self.header,
247	                self.seqs,
248	                self.slots,
249	                self.slot_size,
250	                self.mask,
251	            ),
252	            MpscConsumer::new(
253	                self.header,
254	                self.seqs,
255	                self.slots,
256	                self.slot_size,
257	                self.capacity,
258	                self.mask,
259	            ),
260	        )
261	    }
262	}
263	
264	/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the
265	/// [`MpscHeader`] it must start with; shared by init / attach.
266	///
267	/// - Same split of duties as the SPSC `header_ptr`: alignment
268	///   and header room here, full-geometry length with the
269	///   caller.
270	fn mpsc_header_ptr(base: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<*const MpscHeader, Error> {
271	    if !(base as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
272	        return Err(Error::Misaligned);
273	    }
274	    if len < size_of::<MpscHeader>() {
275	        return Err(Error::TooSmall);
276	    }
277	    Ok(base as *const MpscHeader)
278	}
279	
280	/// Bytes the seq array occupies, padded up to a cache line so
281	/// the slot array behind it stays line-aligned.
282	///
283	/// - u64: `capacity * 4` reaches `2^32` at the `2^30` cap,
284	///   which would wrap a 32-bit usize.
285	fn seq_bytes(capacity: u32) -> u64 {
286	    (capacity as u64 * size_of::<AtomicU32>() as u64).next_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u64)
287	}
288	
289	/// Byte offset of the slot array: header, then the padded seq
290	/// array.
291	///
292	/// - usize return: callers offset a pointer with it, and only
293	///   after the u64 region-size check has proven the region —
294	///   hence this offset — fits in memory.
295	fn slots_offset(capacity: u32) -> usize {
296	    size_of::<MpscHeader>() + seq_bytes(capacity) as usize
297	}
298	
299	/// Bytes needed for an MPSC region with the given geometry.
300	///
301	/// - Computed in u64 for the same 32-bit wrap reason as the
302	///   SPSC `region_size`.
303	pub fn mpsc_region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> u64 {
304	    size_of::<MpscHeader>() as u64 + seq_bytes(capacity) + slot_size as u64 * capacity as u64
305	}
306	
307	/// Geometry checks for [`MpscRing::init`] / [`MpscRing::attach`].
308	///
309	/// - Slot size as the SPSC ring; capacity additionally capped
310	///   at [`MPSC_MAX_CAPACITY`] for tombstone headroom.
311	fn validate_mpsc_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {
312	    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
313	        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);
314	    }
315	    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > MPSC_MAX_CAPACITY {
316	        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);
317	    }
318	    Ok(())
319	}

322	mod tests {
323	    use super::*;
324	    use crate::Ring;
325	
326	    /// Test region: header + seq line (4 × 4 B padded to 64) +
327	    /// 4 slots × 1 line.
328	    const REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<MpscHeader>() + CACHE_LINE_SIZE + (4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
329	
330	    /// Cache-line-aligned backing store for the tests' rings.
331	    #[repr(C, align(64))]
332	    struct Region([u8; REGION_BYTES]);
333	
334	    impl Region {
335	        fn new() -> Self {
336	            Region([0; REGION_BYTES])
337	        }
338	    }
339	
340	    #[test]
341	    fn mpsc_region_size_accounts_for_seq_array() {
342	        // 4 slots: header 256 + seq 16→64 + slots 256 = 576.
343	        assert_eq!(mpsc_region_size(64, 4), 576);
344	        // 32 slots: seq 128 is already a line multiple.
345	        assert_eq!(
346	            mpsc_region_size(64, 32),
347	            (size_of::<MpscHeader>() + 128 + 32 * 64) as u64
348	        );
349	    }

351	    #[test]
352	    fn mpsc_init_rejects_bad_geometry() {
353	        let mut r = Region::new();
354	        assert_eq!(
355	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 63, 4).err().unwrap(),
356	            Error::BadSlotSize
357	        );
358	        assert_eq!(
359	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 0, 4).err().unwrap(),
360	            Error::BadSlotSize
361	        );
362	        assert_eq!(
363	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 3).err().unwrap(),
364	            Error::BadCapacity
365	        );
366	        assert_eq!(
367	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 0).err().unwrap(),
368	            Error::BadCapacity
369	        );
370	        // Over the MPSC cap (fine for SPSC): tombstone headroom.
371	        assert_eq!(
372	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 1 << 31).err().unwrap(),
373	            Error::BadCapacity
374	        );
375	        assert_eq!(
376	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 8).err().unwrap(),
377	            Error::TooSmall
378	        );
379	        assert_eq!(
380	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0[1..], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),
381	            Error::Misaligned
382	        );
383	        // 32-bit tripwire, as the SPSC test: N * M wraps a
384	        // 32-bit usize; the u64 region size must still reject.
385	        assert_eq!(
386	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 1 << 26, 1 << 6).err().unwrap(),
387	            Error::TooSmall
388	        );
389	        // An SPSC-sized region (no seq line) is too small here.
390	        let spsc_bytes = size_of::<crate::Header>() + 4 * 64;
391	        assert_eq!(
392	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0[..spsc_bytes], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),
393	            Error::TooSmall
394	        );
395	    }

397	    #[test]
398	    fn mpsc_init_seeds_seq_array() {
399	        let mut r = Region::new();
400	        // Dirty the region so the seq stores are observable.
401	        r.0.fill(0xAA);
402	        let ring = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
403	        for i in 0..4 {
404	            // SAFETY: i < capacity; test mirrors init's bounds.
405	            let seq = unsafe { &*ring.seqs.add(i as usize) };
406	            assert_eq!(seq.load(Ordering::Relaxed), i);
407	        }
408	        assert_eq!(ring.slot_size, 64);
409	        assert_eq!(ring.capacity, 4);
410	        assert_eq!(ring.mask, 3);

451	    use crate::{Empty, Full};
452	    use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
453	
454	    /// Test message; two words so a torn write would be
455	    /// visible, `val` doubles as the producer id in threaded
456	    /// tests.
457	    #[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]
458	    #[repr(C)]
459	    struct Msg {
460	        seq: u64,
461	        val: u64,
462	    }
463	
464	    #[test]
465	    fn mpsc_roundtrip_full_empty() {
466	        let mut r = Region::new();
467	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
468	
469	        // Empty at start.
470	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
471	
472	        // Fill all 4 slots, then Full on a single probe.
473	        for i in 0..4u64 {
474	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(
475	                |_| false,
476	                |m| {
477	                    m.seq = i;
478	                    m.val = i * 10;
479	                },
480	            )
481	            .unwrap();
482	        }
483	        assert_eq!(
484	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |_| {}).err().unwrap(),
485	            Full
486	        );
487	
488	        // Drain in order; then empty again.
489	        for i in 0..4u64 {
490	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
491	            assert_eq!(
492	                *msg,
493	                Msg {
494	                    seq: i,
495	                    val: i * 10
496	                }
497	            );
498	            msg.release();
499	        }
500	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
501	
502	        // One more send lands in the masked-around slot 0.
503	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(
504	            |_| false,
505	            |m| {
506	                m.seq = 4;
507	                m.val = 0;
508	            },
509	        )
510	        .unwrap();
511	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
512	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 4);
513	        msg.release();
514	    }
515	
516	    #[test]
517	    fn mpsc_policies_count_and_give_up() {
518	        let mut r = Region::new();
519	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
520	
521	        // Empty ring: the consumer's policy sees 0-based
522	        // attempts and its `false` becomes Err(Empty).
523	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
524	        let err = cons
525	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|attempt| {
526	                seen.push(attempt);
527	                attempt < 2
528	            })
529	            .err()
530	            .unwrap();
531	        assert_eq!(err, Empty);
532	        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);
533	
534	        // Fill the ring: the producer's policy gives up with
535	        // Err(Full), and the failed send has zero footprint —
536	        // the fill closure never ran.
537	        for i in 0..4u64 {
538	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = i).unwrap();
539	        }
540	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
541	        let err = prod
542	            .send_with::<Msg>(
543	                |attempt| {
544	                    seen.push(attempt);
545	                    attempt < 2
546	                },
547	                |_| panic!("fill ran on a full ring"),
548	            )
549	            .err()
550	            .unwrap();
551	        assert_eq!(err, Full);
552	        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);
553	
554	        // With room / messages available the policy is never
555	        // consulted.
556	        let msg = cons
557	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| panic!("policy consulted with a message available"))
558	            .unwrap();
559	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 0);
560	        msg.release();
561	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(
562	            |_| panic!("policy consulted with room available"),
563	            |m| m.seq = 4,
564	        )
565	        .unwrap();
566	    }

568	    #[test]
569	    // Dropping the guard is the behavior under test.
570	    #[allow(clippy::drop_non_drop)]
571	    fn mpsc_abandoned_read_guard_redelivers() {
572	        let mut r = Region::new();
573	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
574	
575	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = 1).unwrap();
576	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
577	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
578	        drop(msg);
579	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
580	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
581	        msg.release();
582	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
583	    }
584	
585	    #[test]
586	    fn mpsc_panicking_fill_tombstones_and_skips() {
587	        let mut r = Region::new();
588	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
589	
590	        // A committed message, a panicked fill, another
591	        // committed message.
592	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = 1).unwrap();
593	        let unwound = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
594	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(
595	                |_| false,
596	                |m| {
597	                    m.seq = 99; // partially filled, then…
598	                    panic!("fill panics");
599	                },
600	            )
601	        }));
602	        assert!(unwound.is_err());
603	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = 2).unwrap();
604	
605	        // The consumer sees 1 then 2; the tombstoned slot is
606	        // released without delivery.
607	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
608	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
609	        msg.release();
610	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
611	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 2);
612	        msg.release();
613	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
614	
615	        // The skipped slot is claimable again: a full lap
616	        // still fits 4 messages.
617	        for i in 3..7u64 {
618	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = i).unwrap();
619	        }
620	        assert!(prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |_| {}).is_err());
621	        for i in 3..7u64 {
622	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
623	            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
624	            msg.release();
625	        }
626	    }
627	
628	    #[test]
629	    fn mpsc_indices_survive_u32_wrap() {
630	        let mut r = Region::new();
631	        let ring = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
632	        // Simulate a long-running ring two commits shy of the
633	        // u32 wrap: indices at MAX-1, and each slot's seq must
634	        // equal the free-running position that will next claim
635	        // it (positions MAX-1, MAX, 0, 1 → slots 2, 3, 0, 1).
636	        let start = u32::MAX - 1;
637	        ring.header.producer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
638	        ring.header.consumer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
639	        for (slot, pos) in [(2u32, u32::MAX - 1), (3, u32::MAX), (0, 0), (1, 1)] {
640	            // SAFETY: slot < capacity; test mirrors init.
641	            unsafe { &*ring.seqs.add(slot as usize) }.store(pos, Ordering::Relaxed);
642	        }
643	        let (prod, mut cons) = ring.split();
644	
645	        // Fill across the wrap, then Full.
646	        for i in 0..4u64 {
647	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = i).unwrap();
648	        }
649	        assert!(prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |_| {}).is_err());
650	
651	        // Drain in order across the wrap.
652	        for i in 0..4u64 {
653	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
654	            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
655	            msg.release();
656	        }
657	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
658	    }

660	    #[test]
661	    fn threaded_mpsc_two_producers() {
662	        // Reduced under Miri: interpreted spin loops are slow.
663	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 100 } else { 50_000 };
664	        const PRODUCERS: u64 = 2;
665	        let mut r = Region::new();
666	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
667	
668	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
669	            for p in 0..PRODUCERS {
670	                let prod = prod.clone();
671	                s.spawn(move || {
672	                    for i in 0..COUNT {
673	                        prod.send_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin, |m| {
674	                            m.seq = i;
675	                            m.val = p;
676	                        })
677	                        .unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
678	                    }
679	                });
680	            }
681	            s.spawn(move || {
682	                // Global arrival order is claim order; only
683	                // per-producer FIFO is promised.
684	                let mut next = [0u64; PRODUCERS as usize];
685	                for _ in 0..PRODUCERS * COUNT {
686	                    let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
687	                    let p = msg.val as usize;
688	                    assert_eq!(msg.seq, next[p], "per-producer order broken");
689	                    next[p] += 1;
690	                    msg.release();
691	                }
692	                assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
693	            });
694	        });
695	    }
696

698	    fn threaded_mpsc_shared_reference_producers() {
699	        // The Sync path: two threads share one &MpscProducer
700	        // instead of cloning.
701	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 100 } else { 50_000 };
702	        let mut r = Region::new();
703	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
704	        let prod = &prod;
705	
706	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
707	            for p in 0..2u64 {
708	                s.spawn(move || {
709	                    for i in 0..COUNT {
710	                        prod.send_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin, |m| {
711	                            m.seq = i;
712	                            m.val = p;
713	                        })
714	                        .unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
715	                    }
716	                });
717	            }
718	            s.spawn(move || {
719	                let mut next = [0u64; 2];
720	                for _ in 0..2 * COUNT {
721	                    let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
722	                    let p = msg.val as usize;
723	                    assert_eq!(msg.seq, next[p]);
724	                    next[p] += 1;
725	                    msg.release();
726	                }
727	            });
728	        });
729	    }

1	//! MPSC producing endpoint: [`MpscProducer`] claims a slot
2	//! position by CAS, a closure fills it in place, and the
3	//! commit happens on closure return — abandonment is
4	//! unrepresentable (see the design doc's "MPSC API: closure
5	//! send").
6	
7	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
8	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
9	
10	use super::{MpscHeader, TOMBSTONE};
11	use crate::{Full, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
12	
13	/// A producing handle: `Clone` one per producing thread, then
14	/// `send_with`.
15	///
16	/// - `send_with` takes `&self` — exclusivity comes from the
17	///   claim CAS, not the borrow, so one handle may also be
18	///   shared by reference.
19	pub struct MpscProducer<'a> {
20	    /// The ring's control block.
21	    header: &'a MpscHeader,
22	    /// Base of the per-slot sequence array.
23	    seqs: *const AtomicU32,
24	    /// Base of the slot array.
25	    slots: *mut u8,
26	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`MpscRing`](super::MpscRing)).
27	    slot_size: u32,
28	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
29	    mask: u32,
30	}
31	
32	impl Clone for MpscProducer<'_> {
33	    /// A second producing handle over the same ring; the claim
34	    /// CAS serializes them.
35	    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
36	        MpscProducer { ..*self }
37	    }
38	}
39	
40	// SAFETY: any number of producers is the protocol contract —
41	// the shared state (producer_idx, seqs) is atomic, slot claims
42	// are exclusive by CAS, and slot writes are handed off with
43	// Release/Acquire ordering.
44	unsafe impl Send for MpscProducer<'_> {}
45	// SAFETY: send_with is &self and every access is protected as
46	// above, so shared references across threads are equally fine.
47	unsafe impl Sync for MpscProducer<'_> {}
48	
49	/// Commits-on-drop guard armed while the fill closure runs: a
50	/// panic unwinding through `send_with` must not abandon the
51	/// claimed position (the consumer would wait there forever),
52	/// so drop-without-disarm publishes the tombstoned commit the
53	/// consumer releases without delivering.
54	struct TombstoneOnUnwind<'s> {
55	    /// The claimed slot's seq word.
56	    seq: &'s AtomicU32,
57	    /// The commit value (`pos + 1`).
58	    commit: u32,
59	}
60	
61	impl Drop for TombstoneOnUnwind<'_> {
62	    /// Unwind path only — the normal path disarms with
63	    /// `mem::forget`.
64	    fn drop(&mut self) {
65	        // Release: the (garbage) slot bytes must still be a
66	        // well-defined handoff; the consumer skips the message
67	        // but touches the seq protocol state.
68	        self.seq
69	            .store(self.commit.wrapping_add(TOMBSTONE), Ordering::Release);
70	    }
71	}
72	
73	impl<'a> MpscProducer<'a> {
74	    /// Build the handle from [`MpscRing::split`](super::MpscRing::split)'s
75	    /// geometry snapshot.
76	    pub(super) fn new(
77	        header: &'a MpscHeader,
78	        seqs: *const AtomicU32,
79	        slots: *mut u8,
80	        slot_size: u32,
81	        mask: u32,
82	    ) -> Self {
83	        MpscProducer {
84	            header,
85	            seqs,
86	            slots,
87	            slot_size,
88	            mask,
89	        }
90	    }
91	
92	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line — same contract as
93	    /// the SPSC endpoints' `user()`.
94	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
95	        &self.header.user
96	    }
97	
98	    /// The seq word for free-running position `idx`.
99	    fn seq(&self, idx: u32) -> &AtomicU32 {
100	        // SAFETY: idx is masked to < capacity, inside the seq
101	        // array validated at init/attach.
102	        unsafe { &*self.seqs.add((idx & self.mask) as usize) }
103	    }
104	
105	    /// Claim the next slot position, fill it in place, commit
106	    /// on closure return; retry a full ring under the injected
107	    /// wait policy → [`Full`].
108	    ///
109	    /// - `fill` writes the message through `&mut T`; commit is
110	    ///   by construction — there is no abandonment state (an
111	    ///   SPSC-style guard could be dropped, and in MPSC an
112	    ///   abandoned *claim* wedges the queue).
113	    /// - `on_full` is called after each failed attempt with
114	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
115	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`. Pass `|_| false` for
116	    ///   a single non-blocking probe. A send that returns
117	    ///   `Full` has zero protocol footprint (the overflow-FIFO
118	    ///   seam — see the design doc's "Overflow readiness").
119	    /// - Losing a claim race to another producer is *not* a
120	    ///   policy call: the system made progress; this producer
121	    ///   just retries with the fresher index.
122	    /// - If `fill` panics, the unwind publishes a tombstoned
123	    ///   commit: the consumer releases the slot without
124	    ///   delivering it, and the queue keeps flowing.
125	    pub fn send_with<T>(
126	        &self,
127	        mut on_full: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
128	        fill: impl FnOnce(&mut T),
129	    ) -> Result<(), Full>
130	    where
131	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
132	    {
133	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
134	        let mut pos = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
135	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
136	        loop {
137	            // Acquire pairs with the consumer's Release in
138	            // release(): a claimable seq means the previous
139	            // lap's reads are done.
140	            let seq = self.seq(pos).load(Ordering::Acquire);
141	            let diff = seq.wrapping_sub(pos) as i32;
142	            if diff == 0 {
143	                // Claimable. Weak CAS: a spurious failure just
144	                // retries with the fresher index.
145	                match self.header.producer_idx.compare_exchange_weak(
146	                    pos,
147	                    pos.wrapping_add(1),
148	                    Ordering::Relaxed,
149	                    Ordering::Relaxed,
150	                ) {
151	                    Ok(_) => break,
152	                    Err(actual) => pos = actual,
153	                }
154	            } else if diff < 0 {
155	                // Slot's previous occupant not yet released.
156	                // Re-read the index first: if it moved, this
157	                // was a stale pos (e.g. a tombstoned slot we
158	                // raced), not a full ring — no policy call.
159	                let cur = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
160	                if cur != pos {
161	                    pos = cur;
162	                    continue;
163	                }
164	                if !on_full(attempt) {
165	                    return Err(Full);
166	                }
167	                attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
168	            } else {
169	                // Another producer claimed pos; ours is stale.
170	                pos = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
171	            }
172	        }
173	        // pos is exclusively ours until the seq commit store.
174	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, pos, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;
175	        let commit = pos.wrapping_add(1);
176	        let guard = TombstoneOnUnwind {
177	            seq: self.seq(pos),
178	            commit,
179	        };
180	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
181	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
182	        // (FromBytes bound), and the claim CAS gives exclusive
183	        // slot access until the commit store below.
184	        fill(unsafe { &mut *msg });
185	        // Normal path: disarm the unwind guard, then publish.
186	        let seq = guard.seq;
187	        core::mem::forget(guard);
188	        // Release pairs with the consumer's Acquire seq load:
189	        // observing pos + 1 means the filled bytes are visible.
190	        seq.store(commit, Ordering::Release);
191	        Ok(())
192	    }
193	}
194	
```

1	//! MPSC consuming endpoint: [`MpscConsumer`] reserves the
2	//! oldest committed slot through the [`MpscReadSlot`] guard —
3	//! the SPSC consumer's shape, plus tombstone skipping (an
4	//! unwound producer's slot is released without delivery).
5	
6	use core::marker::PhantomData;
7	use core::ops::Deref;
8	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
9	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, KnownLayout};
10	
11	use super::{MpscHeader, TOMBSTONE};
12	use crate::{Empty, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
13	
14	/// The consuming handle: single per ring, CAS-free —
15	/// `reserve_slot_with` the oldest committed slot, read in
16	/// place, `release`.
17	pub struct MpscConsumer<'a> {
18	    /// The ring's control block.
19	    header: &'a MpscHeader,
20	    /// Base of the per-slot sequence array.
21	    seqs: *const AtomicU32,
22	    /// Base of the slot array.
23	    slots: *mut u8,
24	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`MpscRing`](super::MpscRing)).
25	    slot_size: u32,
26	    /// Geometry snapshot; release stores `pos + capacity`.
27	    capacity: u32,
28	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
29	    mask: u32,
30	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
31	}
32	
33	// SAFETY: the handle owns the single-consumer role; shared
34	// state (seqs, indices) is atomic with Release/Acquire
35	// handoff.
36	unsafe impl Send for MpscConsumer<'_> {}
37	
38	impl<'a> MpscConsumer<'a> {
39	    /// Build the handle from [`MpscRing::split`](super::MpscRing::split)'s
40	    /// geometry snapshot.
41	    pub(super) fn new(
42	        header: &'a MpscHeader,
43	        seqs: *const AtomicU32,
44	        slots: *mut u8,
45	        slot_size: u32,
46	        capacity: u32,
47	        mask: u32,
48	    ) -> Self {
49	        MpscConsumer {
50	            header,
51	            seqs,
52	            slots,
53	            slot_size,
54	            capacity,
55	            mask,
56	            _region: PhantomData,
57	        }
58	    }
59	
60	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line — same contract as
61	    /// the SPSC endpoints' `user()`.
62	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
63	        &self.header.user
64	    }
65	
66	    /// The seq word for free-running position `idx`.
67	    fn seq(&self, idx: u32) -> &AtomicU32 {
68	        // SAFETY: idx is masked to < capacity, inside the seq
69	        // array validated at init/attach.
70	        unsafe { &*self.seqs.add((idx & self.mask) as usize) }
71	    }
72	
73	    /// Reserve the oldest committed slot as a `&T`, applying
74	    /// an injected wait policy: retry until a message arrives
75	    /// or the policy gives up → [`Empty`].
76	    ///
77	    /// - The guard behaves as the SPSC
78	    ///   [`ReadSlot`](crate::ReadSlot): drop without
79	    ///   [`MpscReadSlot::release`] re-delivers the same slot.
80	    /// - Tombstoned slots (a producer panicked mid-fill) are
81	    ///   released and skipped inline; skipping is progress,
82	    ///   not an attempt, so the policy is not consulted for
83	    ///   them.
84	    /// - `on_empty` is called after each failed attempt with
85	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
86	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Empty)`. Pass `|_| false`
87	    ///   for a single non-blocking probe.
88	    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
89	        &mut self,
90	        mut on_empty: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
91	    ) -> Result<MpscReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
92	    where
93	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
94	    {
95	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
96	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
97	        // Ours alone: producers never read it, only we store it.
98	        let mut c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
99	        loop {
100	            let expected = c.wrapping_add(1);
101	            // Acquire pairs with the producer's Release commit:
102	            // observing expected means the fill is visible.
103	            let seq = self.seq(c).load(Ordering::Acquire);
104	            let diff = seq.wrapping_sub(expected);
105	            if diff == 0 {
106	                break;
107	            }
108	            if diff == TOMBSTONE {
109	                // Unwound producer: release the slot without
110	                // delivering and move on.
111	                self.header.consumer_idx.store(expected, Ordering::Relaxed);
112	                self.seq(c)
113	                    .store(c.wrapping_add(self.capacity), Ordering::Release);
114	                c = expected;
115	                continue;
116	            }
117	            // Not committed yet (or a peer-corrupted seq —
118	            // degrade toward Empty, never toward reading an
119	            // unowned slot).
120	            if !on_empty(attempt) {
121	                return Err(Empty);
122	            }
123	            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
124	        }
125	        // Raw pointer, not `&T` — same argument-protector
126	        // rationale as the SPSC ReadSlot.
127	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;
128	        Ok(MpscReadSlot {
129	            header: self.header,
130	            seq: self.seq(c),
131	            msg,
132	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
133	            released_seq: c.wrapping_add(self.capacity),
134	            _slot: PhantomData,
135	        })
136	    }
137	}
138	
139	/// A reserved read slot: `Deref` to read the message, then
140	/// [`release`](MpscReadSlot::release).
141	pub struct MpscReadSlot<'c, T> {
142	    /// The ring's control block (for the consumer_idx store).
143	    header: &'c MpscHeader,
144	    /// The reserved slot's seq word (for the release store).
145	    seq: &'c AtomicU32,
146	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
147	    /// see the SPSC `ReadSlot`.
148	    msg: *const T,
149	    /// Value `consumer_idx` takes on release.
150	    next_idx: u32,
151	    /// Value the seq takes on release (`pos + capacity`):
152	    /// claimable again one lap later.
153	    released_seq: u32,
154	    /// Owns the `&'c` borrow of the consumer.
155	    _slot: PhantomData<&'c T>,
156	}
157	
158	impl<T> Deref for MpscReadSlot<'_, T> {
159	    type Target = T;
160	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
161	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
162	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
163	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
164	        // (FromBytes bound), and the seq protocol gives this
165	        // guard read access until release.
166	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
167	    }
168	}
169	
170	impl<T> MpscReadSlot<'_, T> {
171	    /// Free the slot for reuse.
172	    ///
173	    /// - `consumer_idx` first (`Relaxed` — consumer-private
174	    ///   resume state, racing readers of it don't exist), the
175	    ///   seq store last (`Release` — the protocol-visible
176	    ///   handoff producers acquire). A crash between the two
177	    ///   is outside the contract, as any torn-state crash is.
178	    pub fn release(self) {
179	        self.header
180	            .consumer_idx
181	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Relaxed);
182	        self.seq.store(self.released_seq, Ordering::Release);
183	    }
184	}
185

1	//! Producing endpoint: [`Producer`] reserves the next free
2	//! slot, the [`WriteSlot`] guard writes it in place and
3	//! commits.
4	
5	use core::marker::PhantomData;
6	use core::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};
7	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
8	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
9	
10	use crate::{Header, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
11	
12	/// `reserve_slot_with` failed: every slot holds an
13	/// uncommitted-or-unread message.
14	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
15	pub struct Full;
16	
17	/// The producing endpoint: `reserve_slot_with`, write in
18	/// place, `commit`.
19	pub struct Producer<'a> {
20	    /// The ring's control block.
21	    header: &'a Header,
22	    /// Base of the slot array.
23	    slots: *mut u8,
24	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
25	    slot_size: u32,
26	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
27	    capacity: u32,
28	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
29	    mask: u32,
30	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
31	}
32	
33	// SAFETY: the handle owns the producer role; the shared state it
34	// touches (indices) is atomic, and slot writes are handed off
35	// with Release/Acquire ordering.
36	unsafe impl Send for Producer<'_> {}
37	
38	impl<'a> Producer<'a> {
39	    /// Build the handle from [`Ring::split`](crate::Ring::split)'s
40	    /// geometry snapshot.
41	    pub(crate) fn new(
42	        header: &'a Header,
43	        slots: *mut u8,
44	        slot_size: u32,
45	        capacity: u32,
46	        mask: u32,
47	    ) -> Self {
48	        Producer {
49	            header,
50	            slots,
51	            slot_size,
52	            capacity,
53	            mask,
54	            _region: PhantomData,
55	        }
56	    }
57	
58	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line: zeroed at init,
59	    /// never touched by the crate again.
60	    ///
61	    /// - Shared with the peer: treat contents as untrusted
62	    ///   data — store values, never addresses to dereference.
63	    /// - See the design doc's "Blocking and user words" for
64	    ///   the wakeup-protocol contract it exists to host.
65	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
66	        &self.header.user
67	    }
68	
69	    /// Reserve the next free slot as a `&mut T`, applying an
70	    /// injected wait policy: retry until a slot frees up or
71	    /// the policy gives up → [`Full`].
72	    ///
73	    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard
74	    ///   holds the `&mut Producer` borrow, so a second
75	    ///   reservation before the guard is dropped or committed
76	    ///   does not compile.
77	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`WriteSlot::commit`]
78	    ///   abandons the reservation (nothing published).
79	    /// - `on_full` is called after each failed attempt with
80	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
81	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`. Pass `|_| false`
82	    ///   for a single non-blocking probe.
83	    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard
84	    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying could not return
85	    ///   it): `producer_idx` is ours and loaded once; only
86	    ///   `consumer_idx` is re-read per attempt.
87	    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies
88	    ///   and the composition model.
89	    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
90	        &mut self,
91	        mut on_full: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
92	    ) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>
93	    where
94	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
95	    {
96	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
97	        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.
98	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
99	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
100	        loop {
101	            // `>=`, not `==`: a peer-corrupted consumer_idx
102	            // makes occupancy look huge — fail Full, never hand
103	            // out a slot the protocol doesn't own.
104	            let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
105	            if p.wrapping_sub(c) < self.capacity {
106	                break;
107	            }
108	            if !on_full(attempt) {
109	                return Err(Full);
110	            }
111	            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
112	        }
113	        // Raw pointer, not `&mut T`: a reference field would be
114	        // argument-protected for the whole `commit(self)` call,
115	        // while commit's Release store lets the consumer read
116	        // the slot inside that window — UB (Miri-verified).
117	        // Deref mints short-lived references instead.
118	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;
119	        Ok(WriteSlot {
120	            header: self.header,
121	            msg,
122	            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),
123	            _slot: PhantomData,
124	        })
125	    }
126	}
127	
128	/// A reserved write slot: `DerefMut` to write the message, then
129	/// [`commit`](WriteSlot::commit).
130	pub struct WriteSlot<'p, T> {
131	    /// The ring's control block (for the commit store).
132	    header: &'p Header,
133	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
134	    /// see the comment in [`Producer::reserve_slot_with`].
135	    msg: *mut T,
136	    /// Value `producer_idx` takes on commit.
137	    next_idx: u32,
138	    /// Owns the `&'p mut` borrow of the producer.
139	    _slot: PhantomData<&'p mut T>,
140	}
141	
142	impl<T> Deref for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
143	    type Target = T;
144	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
145	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
146	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
147	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
148	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot_with), and the index
149	        // protocol gives this guard exclusive slot access until
150	        // commit.
151	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
152	    }
153	}
154	
155	impl<T> DerefMut for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
156	    /// Write access to the in-slot message.
157	    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {
158	        // SAFETY: as in deref; &mut self gives exclusivity of
159	        // the minted reference.
160	        unsafe { &mut *self.msg }
161	    }
162	}
163	
164	impl<T> WriteSlot<'_, T> {
165	    /// Publish the slot to the consumer
166	    /// (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
167	    pub fn commit(self) {
168	        self.header
169	            .producer_idx
170	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
171	    }
172	}
173

2	//! slot, the [`ReadSlot`] guard reads it in place and releases.
3	
4	use core::marker::PhantomData;
5	use core::ops::Deref;
6	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
7	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, KnownLayout};

9	use crate::{Header, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
10	
11	/// `reserve_slot_with` failed: no unread messages.

13	pub struct Empty;
14	
15	/// The consuming endpoint: `reserve_slot_with` the oldest
16	/// unread slot, read in place, `release`.
17	pub struct Consumer<'a> {
18	    /// The ring's control block.

20	    /// Base of the slot array.
21	    slots: *mut u8,
22	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).

24	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
25	    mask: u32,

27	}
28	
29	// SAFETY: the handle owns the consumer role; see the Producer
30	// Send rationale.
31	unsafe impl Send for Consumer<'_> {}
32	
33	impl<'a> Consumer<'a> {
34	    /// Build the handle from [`Ring::split`](crate::Ring::split)'s
35	    /// geometry snapshot.
36	    pub(crate) fn new(header: &'a Header, slots: *mut u8, slot_size: u32, mask: u32) -> Self {
37	        Consumer {
38	            header,
39	            slots,
40	            slot_size,
41	            mask,
42	            _region: PhantomData,
43	        }
44	    }
45	
46	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line: zeroed at init,
47	    /// never touched by the crate again.
48	    ///
49	    /// - Shared with the peer: treat contents as untrusted
50	    ///   data — store values, never addresses to dereference.
51	    /// - See the design doc's "Blocking and user words" for
52	    ///   the wakeup-protocol contract it exists to host.
53	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
54	        &self.header.user
55	    }
56	
57	    /// Reserve the oldest unread slot as a `&T`, applying an
58	    /// injected wait policy: retry until a message arrives or
59	    /// the policy gives up → [`Empty`].
60	    ///
61	    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard
62	    ///   holds the `&mut Consumer` borrow, so a second
63	    ///   reservation before the guard is dropped or released
64	    ///   does not compile.
65	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`ReadSlot::release`]
66	    ///   leaves the slot unread — the next reservation
67	    ///   returns it again.
68	    /// - `on_empty` is called after each failed attempt with
69	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
70	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Empty)`. Pass `|_| false`
71	    ///   for a single non-blocking probe.
72	    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard
73	    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying could not return
74	    ///   it): `consumer_idx` is ours and loaded once; only
75	    ///   `producer_idx` is re-read per attempt.
76	    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies
77	    ///   and the composition model.
78	    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
79	        &mut self,
80	        mut on_empty: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
81	    ) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
82	    where
83	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
84	    {
85	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
86	        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.
87	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
88	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
89	        loop {
90	            let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
91	            if p != c {
92	                break;
93	            }
94	            if !on_empty(attempt) {
95	                return Err(Empty);
96	            }
97	            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
98	        }
99	        // Raw pointer, not `&T`: release's store frees the slot
100	        // for producer writes while a reference field would
101	        // still be argument-protected. Deref mints short-lived
102	        // references instead.
103	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;
104	        Ok(ReadSlot {
105	            header: self.header,
106	            msg,
107	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
108	            _slot: PhantomData,
109	        })
110	    }
111	}

132	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
133	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot_with), and the index
134	        // protocol gives this guard read access until release.
135	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
136	    }
137	}

> AGENT

Read the full picture. Here's the education, grounded in the code.

## The SPSC baseline: two single-owner indices

The SPSC ring (`src/producer.rs`, `src/consumer.rs`) has two `AtomicU32` counters in the header, and the whole protocol rests on **each being written by exactly one side**:

- `producer_idx` — free-running count of slots *reserved*. Producer writes it (Release, on `commit`, `producer.rs:167`); consumer only reads it (Acquire, to check non-empty, `consumer.rs:90`).
- `consumer_idx` — free-running count of slots *released*. Consumer writes it (Release, `consumer.rs:141`); producer only reads it (Acquire, to check non-full, `producer.rs:104`).

Because each counter has a single writer, neither side needs a CAS. The producer *owns* `producer_idx` outright — it loads it once (`producer.rs:98`, "Ours alone: no peer moves it") and just stores `p+1` to publish. Occupancy is `p - c`.

That single-writer assumption is exactly what a second producer would break: two producers both read `p`, both write slot `p`, both store `p+1` — one message is silently lost. SPSC can't grow a producer without changing the protocol.

## What MPSC changes to admit many producers

Two structural changes (`src/mpsc/`):

**1. `producer_idx` becomes a CAS-claimed ticket counter.** A producer no longer owns it. It reads `pos`, and to claim that slot it must win a `compare_exchange_weak(pos, pos+1)` (`mpsc/producer.rs:145`). Winner gets exclusive use of slot `pos`; losers get the fresher `pos` back and retry. That CAS is the serialization point among producers — it's the *only* thing producers contend on with each other, and it's why the module raises the atomics floor to `target_has_atomic="32"` (`mpsc/mod.rs:16`).

**2. A per-slot `seq` array replaces "read the other side's index" for publication.** This is the subtler half. Under concurrent producers, *claim order and commit order differ* — producer A can win slot 5 but still be filling it when producer B finishes slot 6. So a single "producer_idx = how many are committed" counter no longer works; slot 6 being done doesn't mean slot 5 is. Each slot gets its own publication word, `seq[pos]` (`mpsc/mod.rs:113`), seeded to `seq[i]=i` at init (`mpsc/mod.rs:165-169`). The lifecycle of one slot's seq word:

- **claimable** at position `pos` when `seq[pos] == pos` (`producer.rs:141-142`, `diff == 0`).
- **committed / readable** when the producer stores `seq[pos] = pos+1` (Release, `producer.rs:190`). The consumer sees it when `seq[c] == c+1` (`consumer.rs:104-105`).
- **released / claimable next lap** when the consumer stores `seq[c] = c+capacity` (Release, `consumer.rs:182`) — i.e. the position that will next reach this slot, one lap later.

**Fullness now comes from the seq, not from `consumer_idx`.** A producer that finds `seq[pos] - pos < 0` (`producer.rs:154`) knows the slot's previous occupant hasn't been released yet — that's "full" — *without ever reading `consumer_idx`*. Symmetrically the consumer never reads `producer_idx`; it reads its own `consumer_idx` and the seq. There are two extra wrinkles the seq encoding buys:

- **Tombstones** (`TOMBSTONE = 1<<31`, `mod.rs:56`) — if a producer's fill closure *panics*, an abandoned claim would wedge the queue forever (the consumer waits at that slot). The `TombstoneOnUnwind` guard (`producer.rs:54-71`) instead commits `pos+1+TOMBSTONE`; the consumer recognizes that exact value (`consumer.rs:108`) and releases the slot without delivering. This is why capacity is capped at `2^30` not `2^31` (`mod.rs:44`) — headroom so the tombstone offset stays unambiguous.
- **Stale-claim detection** — a producer seeing `diff < 0` re-reads `producer_idx` first (`producer.rs:159`); if it moved, this was a tombstoned/raced slot, not a genuine full ring, so no policy call.

The consumer stays **completely CAS-free** (`mpsc/consumer.rs:14`) — single consumer, so it just walks `consumer_idx` forward.

## Why this sets up question #3

Here's the crux that makes MPSC-2t beat SPSC-2t, and it falls right out of the above. Count the cache lines that **bounce between the producer core and consumer core** per message:

**SPSC:** *both* index lines cross-bounce every handoff.
- `producer_idx`: producer writes → consumer reads → line transfers producer→consumer.
- `consumer_idx`: consumer writes → producer reads → line transfers consumer→producer.
- Two lines ping-ponging across the core boundary, each and every message.

**MPSC (at 1p/1c, so the claim CAS is uncontended):**
- `producer_idx` is now touched **only by the producer** (its own CAS; the consumer never reads it) → stays hot in the producer's L1, never bounces.
- `consumer_idx` is touched **only by the consumer** → stays in the consumer's L1, never bounces.
- The only word that crosses the boundary is the **slot seq** (producer commits it, consumer reads+releases it) → *one* bouncing line instead of two.

So MPSC, by making each index private and moving the producer↔consumer handshake onto the per-slot seq, **halves the cross-core coherence traffic** on the hot path — at the cost of the seq array (and, when there really are ≥2 producers, CAS contention on `producer_idx`, which the 1p case doesn't pay). That's the mechanism the todo hypothesizes ("SPSC bounces two index lines... MPSC's only shared hot word is the slot seq"), and it lines up with 73.9 vs 100.1 ns.

That's the theory. The open work in #3 is to **confirm it's actually the cache-line bounce** and not something else (branch layout, the CAS being cheaper than expected, etc.) — the todo names two ways: perf cache-transfer counters, and a padded-seq variant to see if seq-line packing matters.

Before I go further — how do you want to proceed on the verification? Options as I see them:

1. **perf counters** — run the existing 1p/1c benchmark under `perf stat` with cache-coherence events (`mem_load_l3_miss_retired.remote_fwd` / HITM-type counters) on both SPSC and MPSC, and compare cross-core transfers per message against the ~26 ns gap.
2. **padded-seq experiment** — one seq per cache line instead of 16-per-line, to isolate false-sharing-within-the-seq-array from the true index-bounce effect.
3. Just wanted the conceptual grounding for now, and we'll scope the experiment next.

Which direction?

688	## feat: mpsc ring sibling primitive
689	
690	Commits: [[37]],[[38]],[[39]],[[40]],[[41]],[[42]]
691	
692	N senders sharing one queue needs a multi-producer ring, and
693	the SPSC hot path (load/store-only, ~2 ns 1t) must not pay
694	for it — so MPSC lands as a separate sibling primitive,
695	measured A/B against SPSC in iiac-perf. Design:
696	[MPSC ring (sibling primitive)](../ring-buffer-design.md#mpsc-ring-sibling-primitive).
697	
698	- Vyukov's bounded MPMC restricted to MP/SC:
699	  - producers CAS-claim `producer_idx`;
700	  - a per-slot seq array publishes each slot independently
701	    (claim order ≠ commit order under concurrency);
702	  - the consumer stays CAS-free, and producers never read
703	    `consumer_idx` — fullness comes from the slot seq.
704	- Closure send (`send_with`) makes claim abandonment
705	  unrepresentable; a panicking fill publishes a tombstoned
706	  commit (`pos + 1 + 2^31`) the consumer releases without
707	  delivering — an unwind cannot wedge the queue.
708	- Own magic (`"ZCM1"`) and layout version; the module is
709	  gated on `target_has_atomic = "32"`. Filing the gate found
710	  bug #1: the pool's free-stack CAS is ungated, so
711	  load/store-only targets already fail to build.
712	- Demo grew mpsc lines at every placement plus the 2p+1c
713	  shape; iiac-perf grew `zcr-mpsc-1t`/`zcr-mpsc-2t` (bench
714	  code in the sibling repo, committed there separately).
715	- Deferred to the design's measurement plan: the 2p+1c
716	  contention bench and the fan-in comparator — the
717	  round-trip harness needs a competing-producer shape
718	  first.
719	
720	### Outcome: the 2t surprise
721	
722	300s adjusted means: `zcr-with-1t` 2.3 ns vs `zcr-mpsc-1t`
723	4.4 ns — the uncontended claim CAS + seq publish costs
724	~2.1 ns, as expected. Cross-thread at 1p/1c the sign flips:
725	`zcr-mpsc-2t` 73.9 ns vs `zcr-with-2t` 100.1 ns — the MPSC
726	protocol measures *faster*, with a wider spread (p10 35.8 /
727	p90 89.9 vs SPSC's tight 88–98). We think the SPSC
728	round-trip bounces two index cache lines per handoff (each
729	side polls the line the other writes) while the MPSC
730	handoff's only shared hot word is the slot seq — neither
731	side ever reads the other's index line. Promoted to a
732	`## Todo` entry (explore via perf cache-transfer counters
733	and/or the padded-seq variant; if confirmed, a seam-word
734	variant might feed back into the SPSC protocol).

6	By default there are chores/chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes

8	The chores-NN files are numbered in sequence; as a chores file grows

  "old_string": "variant might feed back into the SPSC protocol).\n\n### As-built ladder",
  "new_string": "variant might feed back into the SPSC protocol).\n\nThe mechanism sharpened — a per-message count of the cache\nlines that cross the producer↔consumer core boundary. Both\nrings carry two shared index words plus the slots; what\ndiffers is *who reads whom*:\n\n- SPSC bounces both index lines every handoff:\n  - `producer_idx` — producer writes it on commit\n    (`producer.rs` `commit`), consumer reads it on the\n    non-empty check (`consumer.rs` reserve loop) → the line\n    transfers producer→consumer.\n  - `consumer_idx` — consumer writes it on release,\n    producer reads it on the non-full check → the line\n    transfers consumer→producer.\n- MPSC at 1p/1c (the claim CAS uncontended) bounces one:\n  - `producer_idx` is touched *only* by the producer (its\n    claim CAS; the consumer never reads it) → stays hot in\n    the producer's L1.\n  - `consumer_idx` is touched *only* by the consumer\n    (its own resume state; producers read fullness from the\n    seq) → stays hot in the consumer's L1.\n  - only the slot `seq` crosses — the producer commits it\n    (`seq[pos] = pos+1`, Release), the consumer reads then\n    releases it (`seq[c] = c+capacity`, Release).\n\nWe think that halving — two bouncing lines down to one — is\nthe ~26 ns gap. What MPSC trades for it: the seq array's\nfootprint, and, once there are ≥2 producers, CAS contention\non `producer_idx` that the 1p case never pays. The\nper-slot-independent seq is *forced* by concurrency (claim\norder ≠ commit order), but its side effect at 1p/1c is\nretiring one of SPSC's two cross-core reads — which is why\na seam-word SPSC variant (give SPSC a seq-like publish word\nso neither side reads the other's index) is the feedback\npath worth testing.\n\n### As-built ladder"
}

359	## MPSC ring (sibling primitive)
360	
361	Design for the multi-producer single-consumer ring — a
362	sibling type next to the SPSC ring, not a mode of it. The
363	SPSC hot path is one Relaxed load + one Acquire load + one
364	Release store per side; any unified type would put at least a
365	per-slot sequence check into that path. So the primitives
366	stay separate, existing SPSC users pay nothing, and iiac-perf
367	measures the two directly against each other. Anticipated as
368	a one-bullet placeholder in
369	[Pool topology and phasing](#pool-topology-and-phasing); this
370	section is the full design.
371	
372	### MPSC protocol
373	
374	Vyukov's bounded MPMC queue restricted to many-producer /
375	one-consumer: producers CAS-claim `producer_idx`; a per-slot
376	sequence word publishes each slot independently. The seq
377	exists because claim order and commit order differ under
378	concurrency — the consumer must observe commits, not claims.
379	
380	- **Sequence array** — `M × AtomicU32`, `seq[i]` initialized
381	  to `i`. Slot at free-running position `pos` (masked at
382	  access, as SPSC) is:
383	  - claimable when `seq == pos`,
384	  - committed when `seq == pos + 1`,
385	  - released by the consumer storing `seq = pos + M`.
386	- **Producer claim** — load `producer_idx` (Relaxed), load
387	  that slot's seq (Acquire), branch on the wrapping-signed
388	  diff `seq.wrapping_sub(pos) as i32`:
389	  - `== 0` — claimable:
390	    - `compare_exchange_weak(pos, pos + 1)` on
391	      `producer_idx`; success claims the slot exclusively.
392	    - Write the payload in place.
393	    - `seq.store(pos + 1, Release)` commits.
394	  - `< 0` — full:
395	    - The slot's previous occupant is not yet released.
396	    - The `on_full` policy decides retry vs give-up.
397	  - `> 0` — lost the race to another producer:
398	    - Reload `producer_idx` and retry.
399	    - Not a policy call: the system made progress, only
400	      this producer fell behind.
401	- **Consumer** — single, as in SPSC, so its side needs no
402	  CAS:
403	  - wait for `seq == c + 1` (Acquire),
404	  - read the slot,
405	  - store `seq = c + M` (Release),
406	  - advance the private index.
407	- **Producers never read `consumer_idx`**:
408	  - fullness falls out of the slot seq;
409	  - the header's consumer index line is diagnostic
410	    occupancy only.
411	- **Free-running u32 + power-of-two M** carry over
412	  unchanged: `2^32 % M == 0` keeps seq arithmetic and masked
413	  positions wrap-safe.
414	- **Atomic floor** — the claim CAS raises the floor for MPSC
415	  users only:
416	  - the module is gated on `target_has_atomic = "32"`;
417	  - the SPSC ring stays load/store-only (thumbv6m keeps
418	    working).
419	
420	### MPSC API: closure send
421	
422	The producer-side guard is replaced by a closure, because in
423	MPSC an abandoned *claim* wedges the queue — the consumer
424	waits at that position forever — where SPSC's dropped guard
425	was free (`producer_idx` never moved). Abandonment is made
426	unrepresentable rather than handled:
427	
428	- `MpscRing::init(region, slot_size, capacity)` /
429	  `unsafe attach` — mirror the SPSC constructors:
430	  - own magic and layout_version;
431	  - cross-attaching a region of the wrong kind fails
432	    toward `BadMagic`.
433	- `split() -> (MpscProducer, MpscConsumer)` with
434	  `MpscProducer: Clone + Send`:
435	  - one handle per producing thread;
436	  - cross-process producers attach;
437	  - the endpoint claims word models role slots, not a
438	    single producer bit, so N producer claims fit —
439	    already noted in its todo.
440	- `producer.send_with::<T>(on_full, fill)` →
441	  `Result<(), Full>`:
442	  - `fill: impl FnOnce(&mut T)` writes the payload in
443	    place — claim, fill, commit on closure return;
444	  - commit-by-construction — no public abandonment state;
445	  - `on_full(attempt) -> bool` is the same wait-policy
446	    seam as SPSC (`|_| false` = single probe).
447	- `consumer.reserve_slot_with::<T>(on_empty)` →
448	  `ReadSlot` — the SPSC consumer guard survives:
449	  - consumer abandonment is harmless (drop without
450	    release re-delivers the same slot);
451	  - so the guard shape stays.
452	- **Unwind path** — a panic in `fill` must not wedge the
453	  queue:
454	  - the unwind runs an internal guard's Drop, which
455	    tombstones the claimed slot — a marked commit the
456	    consumer releases without delivering;
457	  - sketch: a reserved seq bit masked out of the index
458	    arithmetic (constrains `M <= 2^30`); encoding is an
459	    open question below;
460	  - only unwinding reaches it — panic=abort targets never
461	    do.
462	
463	### Overflow readiness
464	
465	The sender-private overflow FIFO
466	([Overflow FIFO (future)](#overflow-fifo-future)) must
467	compose with MPSC when it lands. Two properties keep that
468	true; both are named requirements so a later protocol tweak
469	cannot silently break them:
470	
471	- **`Full` is a zero-footprint failure**:
472	  - a send returning `Full` leaves no claim, no tombstone,
473	    no protocol residue;
474	  - the protocol gives this by construction — fullness is
475	    observed in the slot seq *before* any CAS, and a lost
476	    CAS is also stateless — but it is a requirement, not
477	    an accident.
478	- **`Full` is the overflow seam**:
479	  - the future MPSC DescSender probes with `|_| false` and
480	    on `Err(Full)` appends the buffer to its own pending
481	    list;
482	  - pending lists stay per-sender — no shared list, no
483	    CAS;
484	  - per-sender draining preserves exactly the per-producer
485	    FIFO order MPSC promises anyway — inter-producer order
486	    was never promised.
487	
488	### MPSC trust
489	
490	Same posture as the SPSC ring: every shared word is
491	untrusted, corruption degrades service and never causes UB.
492	
493	- The seq array joins the untrusted set:
494	  - positions are always masked, so scribbled seqs yield
495	    spurious Full/Empty or garbage-but-valid `T`s
496	    (zerocopy guarantees representation, not sense);
497	  - never out-of-bounds access.
498	- A hostile *producer* peer is new surface relative to
499	  SPSC:
500	  - it can claim and never commit — wedging the queue, a
501	    liveness loss;
502	  - or commit garbage;
503	  - denial of service stays possible, as it always was —
504	    memory safety does not depend on peers.
505	
506	### Fan-in (composition, not a mode)
507	
508	The other standard many-to-one shape: one SPSC ring per
509	producer, the consumer polls N endpoints. Both have a place:
510	
511	- the shared MPSC ring:
512	  - arrival-order fairness ("fair FIFO");
513	  - one place to poll.
514	- fan-in:
515	  - stays load/store-only;
516	  - zero producer-to-producer interference;
517	  - the consumer chooses the service policy (priority,
518	    round-robin, weighted, …).
519	
520	Fan-in is buildable today from shipped SPSC parts; likely
521	both shapes get offered eventually (a fan-in helper as a
522	later convenience — no commitment yet). The bench matrix
523	measures both from the start.
524	
525	### MPSC measurement plan
526	
527	iiac-perf (sibling repo) grows an mpsc set alongside `zcr`:
528	
529	- `zcr-mpsc-1t` — same-thread round-trip: pure protocol
530	  overhead (CAS + seq vs load/store), against
531	  `zcr-with-1t`.
532	- `zcr-mpsc-2t` — 1 producer / 1 consumer cross-core: what
533	  MPSC costs when you don't need it, against `zcr-with-2t`.
534	- `zcr-mpsc-3t` / `-5t` — 2 / 4 producers, one consumer:
535	  claim-contention scaling, the numbers SPSC cannot
536	  produce.
537	- Fan-in comparator at the same producer counts (N SPSC
538	  rings, round-robin poll).
539	- We think the shared ring:
540	  - loses to fan-in on raw throughput under producer
541	    contention (CAS retries vs disjoint cache lines);
542	  - wins on consumer-side latency and fairness at larger
543	    N.
544	- The matrix exists to check that, not assume it.
545	
546	### MPSC open questions
547	
548	- **Seq array padding**:
549	  - packed `M × 4` bytes — Vyukov's shape; adjacent-slot
550	    false sharing;
551	  - line-padded — `M × 64` bytes of overhead;
552	  - start packed, and the bench matrix decides whether a
553	    padded variant earns a layout flag.
554	- **Tombstone encoding**:
555	  - reserved seq bit — masked arithmetic, `M <= 2^30`;
556	  - or a per-slot side word;
557	  - only the unwind path needs it, so the simplest
558	    correct encoding wins.
559	- **Header consumer index**:
560	  - keep as diagnostic occupancy (Relaxed stores by the
561	    consumer), or drop the line entirely;
562	  - the `occupancy()` polish todo argues for keeping it.
563	
```

21	## Terminology
22	
23	The vocabulary the rest of the document builds on, defined
24	once before first use:
25	
26	- **slot** — a cache-line-aligned `[u8; N]`: one of the
27	  ring's M fixed-size buffers. Untyped storage — a message
28	  is a zerocopy `&T` / `&mut T` view into it (geometry
29	  details in [Constraints](#constraints)).
30	- **reserve** — take exclusive access to a slot through a
31	  guard:
32	  - the producer reserves the next free slot
33	    (`WriteSlot`);
34	  - the consumer reserves the oldest unread slot
35	    (`ReadSlot`).
36	- **commit** — the producer publishes its slot to the
37	  consumer; the handoff of ownership away from the writer:
38	  - SPSC: the `producer_idx` Release store;
39	  - MPSC: the slot's seq Release store.
40	- **release** — the consumer hands its slot back for
41	  reuse; the matching handoff in the other direction:
42	  - SPSC: the `consumer_idx` Release store;
43	  - MPSC: the slot's seq Release store (`pos + M`).
44	- **claim** — MPSC only: a producer wins exclusive
45	  ownership of a slot position by CAS on the shared
46	  producer index; writing and committing follow. Claim
47	  order and commit order can differ — see
48	  [MPSC ring (sibling primitive)](#mpsc-ring-sibling-primitive).
49	
50	## Requirements

585	
586	The layer's rules at a glance; each bullet links to the
587	section that expands it.
588	
589	- A process may have one or more pools
590	  [details](#pool-topology-and-phasing).
591	  - Each pool has one owning allocator: a single thread
592	    allocates, any holder frees.
593	- Not all pools in a process need to be in shared memory
594	  [details](#layer-requirements).
595	  - A process-private pool serves intra-process messaging.
596	- A message to be sent to another process must come from a
597	  pool in shared memory
598	  [details](#pool-id-resolution).
599	  - The receiving process must have that pool mapped.
600	- Queues carry descriptors, not payloads
601	  [details](#descriptors).
602	  - A descriptor is a uniform small
603	    `(pool id, buffer index)` regardless of message size.
604	  - The payload stays at rest in its pool buffer.
605	- Any message travels over any queue
606	  [details](#layer-requirements).
607	  - Precondition: both endpoints have the message's pool
608	    registered.
609	- Each process keeps its own pool registry
610	  [details](#descriptor-and-registry-design-070).
611	  - It maps pool ids to that process's view (mapping) of
612	    each pool.
613	  - Registries are private and never shared; only
614	    descriptors cross process boundaries.
615	  - Pool ids are the shared vocabulary: every participating
616	    process must register a pool under the same id (the
617	    assignment scheme is an open question —
618	    [details](#pool-id-allocation)).
619	- Ownership follows the descriptor
620	  [details](#usage-model-roles-and-buffer-lifecycle).
621	  - alloc → write → send the descriptor over a queue →
622	    the receiver owns it → read, forward over another
623	    queue, or free.
624	  - A buffer is always in exactly one state with one
625	    permitted toucher.
626	- Any holder — any thread or process — may free
627	  [details](#free-path-intrusive-lifo-free-stack).
628	  - The free pushes the buffer directly onto its pool's
629	    free-stack in shared memory — no round-trip through
630	    the owning process.
631	  - The owning allocator finds the buffer on a later
632	    alloc.
633	- Descriptors and free-stack links arriving through shared
634	  memory are untrusted
635	  [details](#trust).
636	  - Both are validated before use.
637	  - Corruption degrades to errors or lost buffers, never
638	    UB.
639	- 0.7.0 builds the in-process slice of this model

641	  - As built, everything is single-process: one address
