> AGENT

Not quite — let me correct that, because it bears on how much the refactor's test net protects us.

**MPSC *is* tested multi-producer** (N=2), two ways:
- `threaded_mpsc_two_producers` (`mod.rs:661`) — `PRODUCERS = 2`, 50,000 messages each (100 under Miri), the consumer draining all `PRODUCERS * COUNT` and asserting **per-producer FIFO** order holds.
- `threaded_mpsc_shared_reference_producers` (`mod.rs:698`) — same, but two threads sharing one `&MpscProducer` (the `Sync` path) instead of clones.

Both run under **Miri** (reduced counts), so the multi-producer path is checked for data races / UB, not just happy-path output. Alongside those are the 1p/1c unit tests (roundtrip/full-empty, policies, tombstone-on-panic, u32-wrap).

So the **correctness** claim isn't 1:1-only. But you're right to be wary, because the coverage has real edges:
- **N = 2 only** — never 3/4/… producers. The claim "N producers" is verified at N=2 and *assumed* to generalize.
- **Stress, not exhaustive** — it's probabilistic thread racing, not model-checked. (The only `loom` item on the board is an *idea*, and it's scoped to SPSC, not MPSC.)
- **Single-process only** — threads sharing memory; no cross-process shared-memory test exists yet.
- **Perf under contention is unmeasured** — the 1p/1c "2t surprise" is all we have; the 2p+1c and higher-N contention benches were explicitly *deferred* in iiac-perf.

Bottom line: enough to trust the refactor preserves MPSC behavior (the 2-producer Miri tests are a solid regression net), **not** enough to bank the "scales to N producers / N:M / all-ISR" claims — those want higher-N tests and ideally a `loom` MPSC model.

**For starting the refactoring cycle:** the reorg is structural, so those existing tests are exactly the safety net — they must keep passing across the move, no behavior change. But before I can write the Preparation (version bump to `0.12.0-0`, In Progress block, plan ladder), we need to settle the **four open questions** I parked in chores-02 — they determine whether this is a pure move or a breaking rename, which shapes the ladder:

1. Public names — flat re-exports vs `spsc::`/`mpsc::` namespacing (breaking)?
2. `registry` — move under `spsc/`, stay top-level, or defer?
3. Shared core — leave in `lib.rs` or lift to a `core`/`common` submodule?
4. Pool — directory now, or leave `pool.rs` a file until it splits?

Want me to put those four to you as a quick decision prompt so we can lock the ladder — and do you want beefed-up MPSC multi-producer testing folded into *this* cycle, or kept as a separate follow-on todo?

> DEVELOPER

First, what stops the current MPSC from being MPMC?

> AGENT

Two things stop it — one fundamental, one a deliberate guardrail.

## The fundamental blocker: the consumer has no *claim*

Recall how the producer side became multi-safe — it **CAS-claims** `producer_idx` (`producer.rs:145`): load the index, verify the slot's `seq`, then `compare_exchange` to win exclusive ownership of that position. Losers retry.

The consumer side has **none of that** (`consumer.rs:98`–`111`, `178`–`183`):
- plain `consumer_idx.load(Relaxed)` → pick slot `c`
- read it
- on release, plain `consumer_idx.store(c+1, Relaxed)` + `seq.store(c+capacity, Release)`

No CAS anywhere. So two consumers would both load the same `c`, both read **the same slot** (double-delivery), and both blindly store `c+1` (a lost update on the index). That's the MPMC break: the dequeue position isn't claimed atomically.

## The guardrail: the API won't even let you try

The single-consumer contract is also enforced in the type system, on purpose:
- `MpscConsumer` is `unsafe impl Send` but **not `Sync`** (`consumer.rs:36`) — you can't share `&MpscConsumer` across threads.
- `reserve_slot_with(&mut self)` — exclusive borrow, one reservation at a time.
- `MpscConsumer` is **not `Clone`** — no second handle.

Compare the producer: `Clone + Send + Sync`, `send_with(&self)` (`producer.rs:32`–`47`). The asymmetry is deliberate — the producer was built shareable, the consumer was locked to one.

## Why it's actually *close* — the seq array is already MPMC

Here's the thing: the per-slot `seq` array **is** the symmetric MPMC mechanism. The design doc even says so — "Vyukov's bounded MPMC queue **restricted to** many-producer / one-consumer." The current code is *half* of Vyukov's MPMC. The seq already supports out-of-order handoff on *both* sides (producers commit out of claim order; consumers could release out of read order the same way). Producer fullness/claim keeps working no matter which consumer releases a slot, because release just sets `seq[pos] = pos + capacity` regardless of who does it.

So MPMC = **give the consumer the mirror of the producer's claim**:
1. CAS-claim `consumer_idx` — load `c`, verify `seq == c+1` (committed), `compare_exchange(c → c+1)`; winner reads slot `c`, then releases `seq = c+capacity`.
2. Make `MpscConsumer: Clone + Sync`, `recv(&self)`.
3. Fold tombstone-skip **into** the claim loop (a consumer that claims a tombstoned slot releases-and-reclaims, instead of the current single-consumer inline advance).
4. **Drop the per-producer-FIFO promise** — MPMC delivers each message once to *some* consumer; there's no per-consumer ordering. (Inherent, not a defect.)

**No new atomics floor** — the consumer CAS is the same `target_has_atomic = "32"` the producer already forces.

## So why MPSC and not MPMC?

It was scoped, not blocked. Single-consumer keeps the **consumer hot path CAS-free** (cheaper), preserves the per-producer ordering guarantee, and matches the common shape (N producers → one service inbox). MPMC trades a consumer-side CAS and all ordering for work-stealing across consumers.

**Relevance to the refactor:** this makes MPMC a natural *fourth* edge primitive — `mpmc/` alongside `spsc/`, `mpsc/`, `pool/` — and it's the direct enabler of the N:M graphs you raised (fan-out to a *pool* of consumers, not just one). Worth keeping in mind as we settle the module layout, since "where would `mpmc/` slot in" is the same question as the sibling-dirs reorg. Doesn't need to be built now — but the module structure should have an obvious empty chair for it.

Want to fold "MPMC as a sibling primitive" into the picture before we lock the refactor's module layout, or keep it as a noted future and proceed with the four layout questions?

> DEVELOPER

I'm thinking we may what to "version" out code so it can be used for comparison purposes.
Should we append a v0, SPSCv0 and rename MPSC to MPSCv0 and any others add v0, thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

I'm thinking we may what to "version" the code so it can be used for comparison purposes.
Should we append a v0, SPSCv0 and rename MPSC to MPSCv0 and any others add v0, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good instinct on the *need* — but I'd push back on the *mechanism*. Let me separate the two.

**The need is real but narrower than "v0 on everything."** What will actually get A/B'd in iiac-perf? Looking at the board: SPSC-baseline vs the **seam-word SPSC** (todo #3), packed-seq vs **padded-seq** MPSC (a layout flag, probably not even a new type), and maybe MPSC vs MPMC. Most of those are **parallel variants or config flags**, not a linear v0 → v1 succession. So a blanket `v0` suffix labels a lineage that mostly won't be linear.

**Three reasons not to suffix the type names now:**
1. **YAGNI churn** — there's exactly one of each today; `RingV0` names a `V1` that doesn't exist, and pollutes every call site and doc example immediately.
2. **"v" implies obsolescence** — for *parallel* forks (baseline vs seam-word), `v0/v1` wrongly reads as "v0 is superseded," when both are live and one is the control. Descriptive names (`spsc::seam`) carry the meaning better.
3. **Rename cost is low here** — pre-1.0, experiment repo (`-x1`), and the only consumer (iiac-perf) is your own sibling repo. A future rename is a local edit, not an ecosystem break. So "avoid future renames" doesn't justify preemptive churn.

**What I'd do instead:** version at the **module path**, never the type name, and keep a clean default alias:
```
spsc::v0::Ring        // explicit, benchmarkable
pub use spsc::v0::Ring as Ring;   // casual users + README use `Ring`
```
When SPSC forks, `spsc::v1::Ring` is a sibling module; iiac-perf pins `spsc::v0::Ring` vs `spsc::v1::Ring`; you flip the `Ring` alias to whichever is "current." Type names stay clean forever; the version lives where versions belong — in the path.

And the real value you're after — a **frozen baseline to measure against** — is best done by *freezing on fork* (copy today's impl into `spsc::v0` at the moment you start `v1`), not by pre-labeling everything now.

This is effectively a **5th layout question** for the refactor, and it interacts with the other four (it's the same "where does a sibling implementation slot in" question as `mpmc/`). Let me put the naming model to you:

Sure — what would you like to clarify? A few things I could sharpen, in case one is the sticking point:

- **The axis itself** — are you picturing *temporal* versions (a frozen v0 baseline vs. an evolving successor) or *parallel* variants (baseline vs. seam-word SPSC, both live)? That changes whether numbers or descriptive names fit.
- **What "coexist" needs to serve** — is the driver iiac-perf linking two implementations into one benchmark run, or just being able to check out an old one from git? (The former needs both in the crate at once; the latter doesn't.)

> DEVELOPER

The thing I don't like your xxx::vN::yy suggestion is the notion of "freezing" when we start a new version it may dead end and we keep it but move back to a previous version. So I don't like the "freeze" wording mechanism I'd want to beable to bounce back and forth. How might we do that?

> AGENT

Ah — the "freeze" was my bad framing, not something the path scheme actually forces. Drop that word entirely. The `spsc::vN::` structure gives you *exactly* the bounce-back-and-forth you want; here's the corrected mental model.

**Versions are peer modules — all live, all editable, none frozen:**
```
src/spsc/
  mod.rs
  v0/   (producer.rs, consumer.rs, mod.rs)
  v1/   (a fork/experiment — an ordinary, editable module)
```
```rust
// spsc/mod.rs
pub mod v0;
pub mod v1;                    // both compile, both always reachable

// the "champion" — ONE line, move it to bounce the default:
pub use v0::{Ring, Producer, Consumer};
```

**Bouncing is a one-line edit.** Prefer v1 today? `pub use v1::{...}`. v1 dead-ends? Change it back to `v0::{...}`. That's the whole operation — the alias is a movable *pointer at the current champion*, not a succession. Nothing is retired, nothing is read-only; `v1` stays a normal module you can keep poking at or return to later.

**Dead-ends cost nothing and stay benchmarkable.** iiac-perf never uses the default alias — it names them explicitly: `zc_ring::spsc::v0::Ring` vs `zc_ring::spsc::v1::Ring`. Both link into the same bench run regardless of which one the champion pointer favors. A dead-ended `v1` just... sits there, still measurable, until *you* decide to delete it (or don't).

So there's no freeze mechanism at all — just N coexisting implementations and one pointer you slide among them.

Two refinements to consider:
- **The default is optional.** You could expose *only* explicit paths (`spsc::v0`, `spsc::v1`) with no champion alias, so every use-site states which version it wants. More ceremony, zero ambiguity. The alias is just convenience sugar for README/examples.
- **If "higher number = better" bothers you** (since v1 might lose to v0), **descriptive names kill that implication**: `spsc::baseline`, `spsc::seam`, `spsc::seam_padded` — an unordered set of named candidates with a champion pointer, no numeric ladder suggesting the newest wins. Same mechanism, friendlier to the "any of them might be the keeper" reality.

One real wrinkle to decide when we lay it out: **what's shared vs. per-version.** v0 and v1 will share most of the crate (`Error`, `slot_ptr`, `CacheAligned`, geometry). Keep that common code in `lib.rs`/a `common` module so each `vN` is only the *protocol delta* (the producer/consumer/ring bits that actually differ) — otherwise versions become near-duplicate copies. A version that needs a different header layout (e.g. seam-word) would own its header; one that only tweaks ordering wouldn't.

Does the movable-champion-pointer model match what you meant by "bounce back and forth"? And do you lean numeric (`v0/v1`) or descriptive (`baseline/seam`) for the module names?

> DEVELOPER

SG overall, but one other refinement, the vX notation per module not necessarily
all modules have to move at once. Of course if their types/API's are different
then they may not be compatible but even that can be handled with conditional
compilation, correct?

> AGENT

Correct on both, with one clarification on what conditional compilation is actually *for* here.

**Per-primitive independent versioning — yes, that falls straight out of the structure.** Because version is the *inner* axis (`spsc::v0`, not `v0::spsc`), each primitive owns its own version set and its own champion pointer. `spsc` can be at v2 while `mpsc` is still v0 and `pool` at v1 — nothing moves in lockstep. Each `{primitive}/mod.rs` slides its own champion line independently.

**Incompatible APIs across versions — they coexist without any cfg.** This is the part worth being precise about: `spsc::v0::Ring` and `spsc::v1::Ring` are just two different types with different methods. Both compile, both link, both are usable side-by-side by explicit path — divergent APIs don't need conditional compilation to coexist. That's the whole payoff of them being separate modules.

**So what cfg is actually for: build-time *inclusion*, not coexistence.** Two real jobs:

1. **Don't pay for versions a build doesn't use.** Gate each version behind a feature so a lean build compiles only its champion, while iiac-perf flips *all* features on to link every version into one bench binary:
   ```toml
   [features]
   default = ["spsc-v0", "mpsc-v0", "pool-v0"]   # current champions
   spsc-v0 = []
   spsc-v1 = []
   mpsc-v0 = []
   ```
   ```rust
   // spsc/mod.rs
   #[cfg(feature = "spsc-v0")] pub mod v0;
   #[cfg(feature = "spsc-v1")] pub mod v1;
   ```
   iiac-perf enables `spsc-v0,spsc-v1`; firmware enables just the one it ships. **This matters most exactly where you're headed** — you don't want three SPSC implementations baked into an M0 firmware image. cfg is how the embedded build stays lean while the bench build stays comprehensive.

2. **Build-time champion selection / mutual exclusion** — if you'd rather pick the default via a feature than a source edit, or if two versions genuinely *can't* coexist (e.g. two `pool` versions each registering a `#[global_allocator]` — only one allowed), cfg makes that a build choice. Niche, but it's there.

So your instinct is right — cfg handles divergent versions fine. Just the framing: coexistence is free (separate modules); cfg is what lets a given build *include only the versions it wants*, which is a code-size/compile-time lever, and a genuinely important one on the M0 side.

One coupling to keep tidy: the champion `pub use vN::…` line has to point at a version the build actually enabled, so the simplest rule is "the champion is always in `default` features." Benches add extra version features *on top of* default; they don't remove the champion.

Does that close the versioning design? If so, this becomes the 5th layout decision and I can fold it into the refactor's plan ladder with the other four.

1. **Structure** — `src/spsc/v0/`, `src/mpsc/v0/`, `src/pool/v0/`. Version is the inner axis, so each primitive versions independently (answers Q4: pool becomes a dir because `pool/v0/` requires it).
2. **Names unchanged** — keep `Ring`, `MpscRing`, `Pool`, … inside `v0`; champion alias per module (`spsc::Ring = spsc::v0::Ring`) + existing crate-root re-exports → **public API identical, non-breaking**. `spsc::v0::Ring` is newly available for iiac-perf pinning. (Dropping the `Mpsc` prefix is a separate breaking change — deferred.)
3. **Shared core** stays in `lib.rs` (crate-wide: `Error`, `slot_ptr`, `CACHE_LINE_SIZE`, …).
4. **registry** stays top-level (placement waits for the descriptor-queue work, Todo #1).
5. **Cargo feature-gating deferred** — coexistence needs no features (separate modules link side by side); gating only pays off once a `v1` exists, so it lands with the first fork. This cycle establishes the *structure*.

```
//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided
//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:
//!
//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`Header`] spanning four cache lines
//!   (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index, app
//!   user words), followed by M slots of N bytes.
//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot
//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.
//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a
//!   [`WriteSlot`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a
//!   [`ReadSlot`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no
//!   serialization step. Each side reserves at most one slot at
//!   a time; the guard holds the endpoint borrow until commit /
//!   release (or drop).
//! - A multi-producer sibling, [`MpscRing`], lives in the
//!   `mpsc` module (gated on CAS support) — see its module
//!   docs for the claim/seq protocol and closure-send API.
//!
//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md

#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]

use core::marker::PhantomData;
use core::mem::{align_of, size_of};
use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
```

The `-0` title breaks the greppable stem.** Ladder titles: `-0` says "module**-**dir" (hyphenated), `-1..-3` say "module dir", close-out "module dirs". `git log --grep "module dir"` would collect every commit *except* the `-0`. **2. `Full`/`Empty` are secretly shared — the plan should say where they land.** They're defined in `producer.rs`/`consumer.rs` (SPSC files), but **MPSC imports them too** (`use crate::{Empty, Full}` in `mpsc/`). If they move into `spsc/v0/` as-is, mpsc — and any future `spsc::v1` — would depend on types living inside *one version* of a sibling primitive. That's exactly the coupling the versioning scheme is meant to prevent. Suggest a decision bullet: **`Full`/`Empty` promote to the shared core in `lib.rs`** (crate-root re-exports keep the public API unchanged). Same logic already holds for `USER_WORDS`, which both headers use — add it to the shared-core list, which currently omits it.

**3. The `-1` scope detail got lost in the chores slim-down.** The deleted provisional plan named exactly which SPSC-specific items leave `lib.rs` (`Header`, `Ring`, `MAGIC`, `LAYOUT_VERSION`, `header_ptr`, `region_size`, `validate_geometry`). 1	//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided
```
2	//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:
3	//!
4	//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`Header`] spanning four cache lines
5	//!   (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index, app
6	//!   user words), followed by M slots of N bytes.
7	//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot
8	//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.
9	//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a
10	//!   [`WriteSlot`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a
11	//!   [`ReadSlot`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no
12	//!   serialization step. Each side reserves at most one slot at
13	//!   a time; the guard holds the endpoint borrow until commit /
14	//!   release (or drop).
15	//! - A multi-producer sibling, [`MpscRing`], lives in the
16	//!   `mpsc` module (gated on CAS support) — see its module
17	//!   docs for the claim/seq protocol and closure-send API.
18	//!
19	//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md
20	
21	#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]
22	
23	use core::marker::PhantomData;
24	use core::mem::{align_of, size_of};
25	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
26	
27	mod consumer;
28	// The MPSC ring needs CAS (the claim), so it is gated; the
29	// SPSC ring protocol stays load/store-only. (The pool's
30	// free-stack also uses CAS and predates the gate — see
31	// notes/bugs.md.)
32	#[cfg(target_has_atomic = "32")]
33	mod mpsc;
34	pub mod policy;
35	mod pool;
36	mod producer;
37	mod registry;
38	
39	pub use consumer::{Consumer, Empty, ReadSlot};
40	#[cfg(target_has_atomic = "32")]
41	pub use mpsc::{MpscConsumer, MpscHeader, MpscProducer, MpscReadSlot, MpscRing, mpsc_region_size};
42	pub use pool::{BufSlot, Exhausted, Pool, PoolHeader, PoolResolver};
43	pub use producer::{Full, Producer, WriteSlot};
44	pub use registry::{Desc, PoolId, PoolRegistry, RegistryError};
45	
46	/// Cache-line size the layout is built around.
47	///
48	/// - Slot size must be a multiple of this; slots and the region
49	///   itself must be aligned to it.
50	pub const CACHE_LINE_SIZE: usize = 64;
51	
52	/// Layout marker written by [`Ring::init`]; rejects foreign
53	/// regions on attach.
54	const MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_5231; // "ZCR1"
55	
56	/// Bumped on any change to the region layout.
57	const LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 2;
58	
59	/// Number of `AtomicU32` words in the header's app-owned
60	/// `user` line.
61	pub const USER_WORDS: usize = 16;
62	
63	/// Cache-line-aligned wrapper granting its field sole
64	/// ownership of the line.
65	///
66	/// - `repr(align(N))` accepts only an integer literal — it
67	///   cannot name [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] — so the `64` is written out
68	///   and a const assert ties them back together.
69	#[repr(C, align(64))]
70	struct CacheAligned<T>(T);
71	
72	const _: () = assert!(align_of::<CacheAligned<AtomicU32>>() == CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
73	
74	impl<T> core::ops::Deref for CacheAligned<T> {
75	    type Target = T;
76	    /// Access the wrapped value.
77	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
78	        &self.0
79	    }
80	}
81	
82	/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning
83	/// four cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its
84	/// own line.
85	///
86	/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`Ring::init`] with `magic`
87	///   last (`Release`), read-only thereafter. Cold: per-op
88	///   paths use the handles' snapshots.
89	/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.
90	/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.
91	/// - line 3: `user`, app-owned scratch — last, so an app
92	///   overrun walks into its own slot data, not an index line.
93	/// - Every field is atomic: the region may be mapped by a peer
94	///   at any time, so even "immutable" fields must be free of
95	///   data races. `AtomicU32` has the same layout as `u32`, so
96	///   this does not affect `layout_version`.
97	#[repr(C)]
98	pub struct Header {
99	    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]); stored last by init
100	    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`), so a
101	    /// peer that observes it also observes the geometry.
102	    magic: AtomicU32,
103	    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).
104	    layout_version: AtomicU32,
105	    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.
106	    slot_size: AtomicU32,
107	    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.
108	    capacity: AtomicU32,
109	    /// [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] this region was built with. The line size
110	    /// is a layout parameter (padding, offsets, slot granule),
111	    /// so builds must agree; attach validates it like the rest
112	    /// of the geometry.
113	    cache_line_size: AtomicU32,
114	    /// Free-running count of messages committed.
115	    producer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
116	    /// Free-running count of messages released.
117	    consumer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
118	    /// App-owned scratch line ([`USER_WORDS`] words): zeroed by
119	    /// init, then never read, written, or interpreted by the
120	    /// crate. See the design doc's "Blocking and user words".
121	    user: CacheAligned<[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS]>,
122	}
123	
124	const _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
125	
126	/// Errors from region validation — [`Ring::init`] /
127	/// [`Ring::attach`] and [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`].
128	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
129	pub enum Error {
130	    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned.
131	    Misaligned,
132	    /// Region is smaller than header + slots/buffers.
133	    TooSmall,
134	    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.
135	    BadSlotSize,
136	    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.
137	    BadCapacity,
138	    /// Pool: buffer size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
139	    /// multiple.
140	    BadBufSize,
141	    /// Pool: buffer count is zero or `u32::MAX` (the
142	    /// free-stack NIL sentinel).
143	    BadBufCount,
144	    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a region of the expected
145	    /// kind.
146	    BadMagic,
147	    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.
148	    BadLayoutVersion,
149	    /// Attach: region built with a different [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`].
150	    BadCacheLine,
151	}
152	
153	/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two
154	/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].
155	///
156	/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at
157	///   init/attach: per-op paths never re-read fields a peer
158	///   could scribble on, so a hostile peer can degrade the ring
159	///   to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty, never to UB.
160	pub struct Ring<'a> {
161	    /// The region's control block.
162	    header: &'a Header,
163	    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).
164	    slots: *mut u8,
165	    /// Snapshot of `header.slot_size`.
166	    slot_size: u32,
167	    /// Snapshot of `header.capacity`.
168	    capacity: u32,
169	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
170	    mask: u32,
171	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
172	}
173	
174	impl<'a> Ring<'a> {
175	    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.
176	    ///
177	    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
178	    ///   multiple.
179	    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^31`.
180	    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned and at least
181	    ///   `size_of::<Header>() + M * N` bytes.
182	    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {
183	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
184	        let len = region.len();
185	        // Taken exactly once: a second `as_mut_ptr()` would
186	        // retag the slice and invalidate `header` under Stacked
187	        // Borrows (Miri-verified).
188	        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();
189	        let header = header_ptr(base, len)?;
190	        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
191	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
192	        }
193	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by
194	        // header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed for 'a;
195	        // any byte pattern is a valid Header (all-atomic
196	        // fields, plain-byte padding).
197	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
198	        header
199	            .layout_version
200	            .store(LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);
201	        header.slot_size.store(slot_size, Ordering::Relaxed);
202	        header.capacity.store(capacity, Ordering::Relaxed);
203	        header
204	            .cache_line_size
205	            .store(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);
206	        header.producer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
207	        header.consumer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
208	        for word in header.user.iter() {
209	            word.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
210	        }
211	        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region must
212	        // never observe MAGIC before the geometry it validates.
213	        // (Re-initializing a region a peer is already attached
214	        // to is outside the contract.)
215	        header.magic.store(MAGIC, Ordering::Release);
216	        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.
217	        let slots = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<Header>()) };
218	        Ok(Ring {
219	            header,
220	            slots,
221	            slot_size,
222	            capacity,
223	            mask: capacity - 1,
224	            _region: PhantomData,
225	        })
226	    }
227	
228	    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)
229	    /// already initialized, validating its header.
230	    ///
231	    /// # Safety
232	    ///
233	    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive
234	    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a
235	    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).
236	    /// - No other producer attaches if this side will produce;
237	    ///   likewise for the consumer side (SPSC contract).
238	    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {
239	        let header = header_ptr(region, len)?;
240	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by
241	        // header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is live and
242	        // shared.
243	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
244	        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic: a
245	        // valid magic means the geometry stores are visible.
246	        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != MAGIC {
247	            return Err(Error::BadMagic);
248	        }
249	        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != LAYOUT_VERSION {
250	            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);
251	        }
252	        if header.cache_line_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32 {
253	            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);
254	        }
255	        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read it.
256	        let slot_size = header.slot_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
257	        let capacity = header.capacity.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
258	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
259	        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
260	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
261	        }
262	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.
263	        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };
264	        Ok(Ring {
265	            header,
266	            slots,
267	            slot_size,
268	            capacity,
269	            mask: capacity - 1,
270	            _region: PhantomData,
271	        })
272	    }
273	
274	    /// Split into the producer and consumer endpoint handles.
275	    ///
276	    /// - Consuming `self` makes each handle exist at most once
277	    ///   per ring per process; cross-process, one producing and
278	    ///   one consuming process is the SPSC contract.
279	    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {
280	        (
281	            Producer::new(
282	                self.header,
283	                self.slots,
284	                self.slot_size,
285	                self.capacity,
286	                self.mask,
287	            ),
288	            Consumer::new(self.header, self.slots, self.slot_size, self.mask),
289	        )
290	    }
291	}
292	
293	/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the `Header`
294	/// it must start with; shared by [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].
295	///
296	/// - Checks alignment and room for the `Header` itself. The
297	///   full-geometry length check stays with the caller: init
298	///   knows the geometry from its parameters, attach only after
299	///   reading it from the returned header.
300	/// - Returns a raw pointer — no deref here. Each caller
301	///   discharges its own safety obligation (init: exclusive
302	///   borrow; attach: the caller's shared-mapping contract).
303	fn header_ptr(base: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<*const Header, Error> {
304	    if !(base as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
305	        return Err(Error::Misaligned);
306	    }
307	    if len < size_of::<Header>() {
308	        return Err(Error::TooSmall);
309	    }
310	    Ok(base as *const Header)
311	}
312	
313	/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.
314	///
315	/// - Computed in u64: the usize product can wrap on 32-bit
316	///   targets (e.g. N = 2^26, M = 2^6), which would validate a
317	///   tiny region and let safe code write out of bounds.
318	fn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> u64 {
319	    size_of::<Header>() as u64 + slot_size as u64 * capacity as u64
320	}
321	
322	/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].
323	fn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {
324	    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
325	        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);
326	    }
327	    // The `> 1 << 31` arm is unreachable (u32 powers of two are
328	    // <= 2^31); it stays to document the M bound the occupancy
329	    // math relies on.
330	    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {
331	        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);
332	    }
333	    Ok(())
334	}
335	
336	/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per `reserve_slot_with`
337	/// (both endpoints).
338	///
339	/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming
340	///   error, not a runtime condition.
341	fn check_type<T>(slot_size: u32) {
342	    assert!(size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize, "T larger than slot");
343	    assert!(
344	        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE_SIZE,
345	        "T alignment exceeds slot alignment"
346	    );
347	}
348	
349	/// Non-panicking form of [`check_type`], for descriptor
350	/// resolve — there the pool compared against is selected by
351	/// untrusted input, which must not be able to select a panic.
352	fn type_fits<T>(slot_size: u32) -> bool {
353	    size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize && core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE_SIZE
354	}
355	
356	/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.
357	///
358	/// - Returned range is `slot_size` bytes, cache-line aligned
359	///   (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).
360	fn slot_ptr(slots: *mut u8, idx: u32, mask: u32, slot_size: u32) -> *mut u8 {
361	    let pos = (idx & mask) as usize;
362	    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot
363	    // array validated at init/attach.
364	    unsafe { slots.add(pos * slot_size as usize) }
365	}
366	
367	#[cfg(test)]
368	mod tests {
369	    use super::*;
370	    use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
371	
372	    /// Test message; two words so a torn write would be visible.
373	    #[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]
374	    #[repr(C)]
375	    struct Msg {
376	        seq: u64,
377	        val: u64,
378	    }
379	
380	    /// Test region size: header + (4 slots × 1 line each).
381	    const REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<Header>() + (4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
382	
383	    /// Cache-line-aligned backing store for the tests' rings.
384	    #[repr(C, align(64))]
385	    struct Region([u8; REGION_BYTES]);
386	
387	    impl Region {
388	        fn new() -> Self {
389	            Region([0; REGION_BYTES])
390	        }
391	    }
392	
393	    #[test]
394	    fn init_rejects_bad_geometry() {
395	        let mut r = Region::new();
396	        assert_eq!(
397	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 63, 4).err().unwrap(),
398	            Error::BadSlotSize
399	        );
400	        assert_eq!(
401	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 0, 4).err().unwrap(),
402	            Error::BadSlotSize
403	        );
404	        assert_eq!(
405	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 3).err().unwrap(),
406	            Error::BadCapacity
407	        );
408	        assert_eq!(
409	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 0).err().unwrap(),
410	            Error::BadCapacity
411	        );
412	        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 8).err().unwrap(), Error::TooSmall);
413	        assert_eq!(
414	            Ring::init(&mut r.0[1..], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),
415	            Error::Misaligned
416	        );
417	        // 32-bit tripwire: N * M wraps a 32-bit usize (2^26 *
418	        // 2^6 = 2^32); the u64 region_size must still reject it.
419	        assert_eq!(
420	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 1 << 26, 1 << 6).err().unwrap(),
421	            Error::TooSmall
422	        );
423	    }
424	
425	    #[test]
426	    fn attach_validates_header() {
427	        let mut r = Region::new();
428	        // Not initialized yet: magic is zero.
429	        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }
430	            .err()
431	            .unwrap();
432	        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadMagic);
433	        Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
434	        let ring = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }.unwrap();
435	        assert_eq!(ring.slot_size, 64);
436	        assert_eq!(ring.capacity, 4);
437	        assert_eq!(ring.mask, 3);
438	        // A region built with a different CACHE_LINE_SIZE (simulated
439	        // by editing the recorded value) is rejected.
440	        ring.header.cache_line_size.store(128, Ordering::Relaxed);
441	        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }
442	            .err()
443	            .unwrap();
444	        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadCacheLine);
445	    }
446	
447	    #[test]
448	    fn user_words_zeroed_and_shared() {
449	        let mut r = Region::new();
450	        // Dirty the region so init's zeroing is observable.
451	        r.0.fill(0xAA);
452	        let ring = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
453	        let (prod, mut cons) = ring.split();
454	        assert!(prod.user().iter().all(|w| w.load(Ordering::Relaxed) == 0));
455	        // Both endpoints see the same line; a producer-side
456	        // store is visible through the consumer's accessor.
457	        prod.user()[0].store(7, Ordering::Release);
458	        assert_eq!(cons.user()[0].load(Ordering::Acquire), 7);
459	        // The user line must not alias ring state: scribbling
460	        // all of it leaves the ring empty and functional.
461	        for w in cons.user().iter() {
462	            w.store(u32::MAX, Ordering::Relaxed);
463	        }
464	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
465	    }
466	
467	    #[test]
468	    fn indices_survive_u32_wrap() {
469	        let mut r = Region::new();
470	        let ring = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
471	        // Simulate a long-running ring two commits shy of the
472	        // u32 wrap (empty state: p == c).
473	        let start = u32::MAX - 1;
474	        ring.header.producer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
475	        ring.header.consumer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
476	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = ring.split();
477	
478	        // Fill: producer_idx runs MAX-1, MAX, 0, 1 — masked
479	        // slot positions 2, 3, 0, 1.
480	        for i in 0..4u64 {
481	            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
482	            slot.seq = i;
483	            slot.val = i;
484	            slot.commit();
485	        }
486	        assert!(prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
487	
488	        // Drain in order across the wrap.
489	        for i in 0..4u64 {
490	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
491	            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
492	            msg.release();
493	        }
494	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
495	    }
496	
497	    #[test]
498	    fn roundtrip_full_empty() {
499	        let mut r = Region::new();
500	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
501	
502	        // Empty at start.
503	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
504	
505	        // Fill all 4 slots, then Full.
506	        for i in 0..4u64 {
507	            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
508	            slot.seq = i;
509	            slot.val = i * 10;
510	            slot.commit();
511	        }
512	        assert!(prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
513	
514	        // Drain in order; wrap: slot 0 is reused by seq 4.
515	        for i in 0..4u64 {
516	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
517	            assert_eq!(
518	                *msg,
519	                Msg {
520	                    seq: i,
521	                    val: i * 10
522	                }
523	            );
524	            msg.release();
525	        }
526	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
527	
528	        // One more write lands in the masked-around slot 0.
529	        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
530	        slot.seq = 4;
531	        slot.commit();
532	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
533	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 4);
534	        msg.release();
535	    }
536	
537	    #[test]
538	    // Dropping the guards is the behavior under test; they have
539	    // no Drop impl by design (abandon = do nothing).
540	    #[allow(clippy::drop_non_drop)]
541	    fn abandoned_guards_publish_nothing() {
542	        let mut r = Region::new();
543	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
544	
545	        // Reserve then drop: nothing published.
546	        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
547	        slot.seq = 99;
548	        drop(slot);
549	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
550	
551	        // Commit one, reserve then drop: still unread, the
552	        // next reservation returns it again.
553	        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
554	        slot.seq = 1;
555	        slot.commit();
556	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
557	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
558	        drop(msg);
559	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
560	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
561	        msg.release();
562	    }
563	
564	    #[test]
565	    fn threaded_spsc() {
566	        // Reduced under Miri: interpreted spin loops are slow,
567	        // and its scheduler explores interleavings at any count.
568	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 200 } else { 100_000 };
569	        let mut r = Region::new();
570	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
571	
572	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
573	            s.spawn(move || {
574	                for i in 0..COUNT {
575	                    loop {
576	                        match prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false) {
577	                            Ok(mut slot) => {
578	                                slot.seq = i;
579	                                slot.val = i * 3;
580	                                slot.commit();
581	                                break;
582	                            }
583	                            Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
584	                        }
585	                    }
586	                }
587	            });
588	            s.spawn(move || {
589	                for i in 0..COUNT {
590	                    loop {
591	                        match cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false) {
592	                            Ok(msg) => {
593	                                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
594	                                assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);
595	                                msg.release();
596	                                break;
597	                            }
598	                            Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
599	                        }
600	                    }
601	                }
602	            });
603	        });
604	    }
605	
606	    #[test]
607	    fn reserve_slot_with_policy_counts_and_gives_up() {
608	        let mut r = Region::new();
609	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
610	
611	        // Empty ring: the consumer's policy sees 0-based
612	        // attempts and its `false` becomes Err(Empty).
613	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
614	        let err = cons
615	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|attempt| {
616	                seen.push(attempt);
617	                attempt < 2
618	            })
619	            .err()
620	            .unwrap();
621	        assert_eq!(err, Empty);
622	        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);
623	
624	        // Fill the ring: the producer's policy gives up with
625	        // Err(Full).
626	        for i in 0..4 {
627	            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
628	            slot.seq = i;
629	            slot.commit();
630	        }
631	        let err = prod
632	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|attempt| attempt < 2)
633	            .err()
634	            .unwrap();
635	        assert_eq!(err, Full);
636	
637	        // With room / messages available the policy is never
638	        // consulted.
639	        let msg = cons
640	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| panic!("policy consulted with a message available"))
641	            .unwrap();
642	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 0);
643	        msg.release();
644	        let mut slot = prod
645	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| panic!("policy consulted with room available"))
646	            .unwrap();
647	        slot.seq = 4;
648	        slot.commit();
649	    }
650	
651	    #[test]
652	    fn threaded_spsc_spin_variants() {
653	        // The threaded_spsc flow through the shipped
654	        // policy::spin on both ends — the shipped-policy model
655	        // in use.
656	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 200 } else { 100_000 };
657	        let mut r = Region::new();
658	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
659	
660	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
661	            s.spawn(move || {
662	                for i in 0..COUNT {
663	                    let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap();
664	                    slot.seq = i;
665	                    slot.val = i * 3;
666	                    slot.commit();
667	                }
668	            });
669	            s.spawn(move || {
670	                for i in 0..COUNT {
671	                    let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap();
672	                    assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
673	                    assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);
674	                    msg.release();
675	                }
676	            });
677	        });
678	    }
679	}
```

```json
  "content": "//! SPSC ring v0: the load/store-only single-producer\n//! single-consumer protocol, per\n//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:\n//!\n//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`Header`] spanning four cache lines\n//!   (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index, app\n//!   user words), followed by M slots of N bytes.\n//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot\n//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.\n//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a\n//!   [`WriteSlot`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a\n//!   [`ReadSlot`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no\n//!   serialization step. Each side reserves at most one slot at\n//!   a time; the guard holds the endpoint borrow until commit /\n//!   release (or drop).\n//!\n//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md\n\nuse core::marker::PhantomData;\nuse core::mem::size_of;\nuse core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};\n\nuse crate::{CACHE_LINE_SIZE, CacheAligned, Error, USER_WORDS};\n\nmod consumer;\nmod producer;\n\npub use consumer::{Consumer, ReadSlot};\npub use producer::{Producer, WriteSlot};\n\n/// Layout marker written by [`Ring::init`]; rejects foreign\n/// regions on attach.\nconst MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_5231; // \"ZCR1\"\n\n/// Bumped on any change to the region layout.\nconst LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 2;\n\n/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning\n/// four cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its\n/// own line.\n///\n/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`Ring::init`] with `magic`\n///   last (`Release`), read-only thereafter. Cold: per-op\n///   paths use the handles' snapshots.\n/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.\n/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.\n/// - line 3: `user`, app-owned scratch — last, so an app\n///   overrun walks into its own slot data, not an index line.\n/// - Every field is atomic: the region may be mapped by a peer\n///   at any time, so even \"immutable\" fields must be free of\n///   data races. `AtomicU32` has the same layout as `u32`, so\n///   this does not affect `layout_version`.\n#[repr(C)]\npub struct Header {\n    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]); stored last by init\n    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`), so a\n    /// peer that observes it also observes the geometry.\n    magic: AtomicU32,\n    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).\n    layout_version: AtomicU32,\n    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.\n    slot_size: AtomicU32,\n    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.\n    capacity: AtomicU32,\n    /// [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] this region was built with. The line size\n    /// is a layout parameter (padding, offsets, slot granule),\n    /// so builds must agree; attach validates it like the rest\n    /// of the geometry.\n    cache_line_size: AtomicU32,\n    /// Free-running count of messages committed.\n    producer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,\n    /// Free-running count of messages released.\n    consumer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,\n    /// App-owned scratch line ([`USER_WORDS`] words): zeroed by\n    /// init, then never read, written, or interpreted by the\n    /// crate. See the design doc's \"Blocking and user words\".\n    user: CacheAligned<[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS]>,\n}\n\nconst _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);\n\n/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two\n/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].\n///\n/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at\n///   init/attach: per-op paths never re-read fields a peer\n///   could scribble on, so a hostile peer can degrade the ring\n///   to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty, never to UB.\npub struct Ring<'a> {\n    /// The region's control block.\n    header: &'a Header,\n    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).\n    slots: *mut u8,\n    /// Snapshot of `header.slot_size`.\n    slot_size: u32,\n    /// Snapshot of `header.capacity`.\n    capacity: u32,\n    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).\n    mask: u32,\n    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,\n}\n\nimpl<'a> Ring<'a> {\n    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.\n    ///\n    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]\n    ///   multiple.\n    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^31`.\n    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned and at least\n    ///   `size_of::<Header>() + M * N` bytes.\n    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;\n        let len = region.len();\n        // Taken exactly once: a second `as_mut_ptr()` would\n        // retag the slice and invalidate `header` under Stacked\n        // Borrows (Miri-verified).\n        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();\n        let header = header_ptr(base, len)?;\n        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by\n        // header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed for 'a;\n        // any byte pattern is a valid Header (all-atomic\n        // fields, plain-byte padding).\n        let header = unsafe { &*header };\n        header\n            .layout_version\n            .store(LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.slot_size.store(slot_size, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.capacity.store(capacity, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header\n            .cache_line_size\n            .store(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.producer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.consumer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        for word in header.user.iter() {\n            word.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        }\n        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region must\n        // never observe MAGIC before the geometry it validates.\n        // (Re-initializing a region a peer is already attached\n        // to is outside the contract.)\n        header.magic.store(MAGIC, Ordering::Release);\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            slot_size,\n            capacity,\n            mask: capacity - 1,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)\n    /// already initialized, validating its header.\n    ///\n    /// # Safety\n    ///\n    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive\n    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a\n    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).\n    /// - No other producer attaches if this side will produce;\n    ///   likewise for the consumer side (SPSC contract).\n    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        let header = header_ptr(region, len)?;\n        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by\n        // header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is live and\n        // shared.\n        let header = unsafe { &*header };\n        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic: a\n        // valid magic means the geometry stores are visible.\n        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != MAGIC {\n            return Err(Error::BadMagic);\n        }\n        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != LAYOUT_VERSION {\n            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);\n        }\n        if header.cache_line_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32 {\n            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);\n        }\n        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read it.\n        let slot_size = header.slot_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let capacity = header.capacity.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;\n        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            slot_size,\n            capacity,\n            mask: capacity - 1,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Split into the producer and consumer endpoint handles.\n    ///\n    /// - Consuming `self` makes each handle exist at most once\n    ///   per ring per process; cross-process, one producing and\n    ///   one consuming process is the SPSC contract.\n    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {\n        (\n            Producer::new(\n                self.header,\n                self.slots,\n                self.slot_size,\n                self.capacity,\n                self.mask,\n            ),\n            Consumer::new(self.header, self.slots, self.slot_size, self.mask),\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the `Header`\n/// it must start with; shared by [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].\n///\n/// - Checks alignment and room for the `Header` itself. The\n///   full-geometry length check stays with the caller: init\n///   knows the geometry from its parameters, attach only after\n///   reading it from the returned header.\n/// - Returns a raw pointer — no deref here. Each caller\n///   discharges its own safety obligation (init: exclusive\n///   borrow; attach: the caller's shared-mapping contract).\nfn header_ptr(base: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<*const Header, Error> {\n    if !(base as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {\n        return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n    }\n    if len < size_of::<Header>() {\n        return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n    }\n    Ok(base as *const Header)\n}\n\n/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.\n///\n/// - Computed in u64: the usize product can wrap on 32-bit\n///   targets (e.g. N = 2^26, M = 2^6), which would validate a\n///   tiny region and let safe code write out of bounds.\nfn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> u64 {\n    size_of::<Header>() as u64 + slot_size as u64 * capacity as u64\n}\n\n/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].\nfn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {\n        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);\n    }\n    // The `> 1 << 31` arm is unreachable (u32 powers of two are\n    // <= 2^31); it stays to document the M bound the occupancy\n    // math relies on.\n    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {\n        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::{Empty, Full};\n    use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};\n\n    /// Test message; two words so a torn write would be visible.\n    #[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]\n    #[repr(C)]\n    struct Msg {\n        seq: u64,\n        val: u64,\n    }\n\n    /// Test region size: header + (4 slots × 1 line each).\n    const REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<Header>() + (4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);\n\n    /// Cache-line-aligned backing store for the tests' rings.\n    #[repr(C, align(64))]\n    struct Region([u8; REGION_BYTES]);\n\n    impl Region {\n        fn new() -> Self {\n            Region([0; REGION_BYTES])\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn init_rejects_bad_geometry() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        assert_eq!(\n            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 63, 4).err().unwrap(),\n            Error::BadSlotSize\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 0, 4).err().unwrap(),\n            Error::BadSlotSize\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 3).err().unwrap(),\n            Error::BadCapacity\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 0).err().unwrap(),\n            Error::BadCapacity\n        );\n        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 8).err().unwrap(), Error::TooSmall);\n        assert_eq!(\n            Ring::init(&mut r.0[1..], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),\n            Error::Misaligned\n        );\n        // 32-bit tripwire: N * M wraps a 32-bit usize (2^26 *\n        // 2^6 = 2^32); the u64 region_size must still reject it.\n        assert_eq!(\n            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 1 << 26, 1 << 6).err().unwrap(),\n            Error::TooSmall\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn attach_validates_header() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        // Not initialized yet: magic is zero.\n        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }\n            .err()\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadMagic);\n        Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();\n        let ring = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }.unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(ring.slot_size, 64);\n        assert_eq!(ring.capacity, 4);\n        assert_eq!(ring.mask, 3);\n        // A region built with a different CACHE_LINE_SIZE (simulated\n        // by editing the recorded value) is rejected.\n        ring.header.cache_line_size.store(128, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }\n            .err()\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadCacheLine);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn user_words_zeroed_and_shared() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        // Dirty the region so init's zeroing is observable.\n        r.0.fill(0xAA);\n        let ring = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();\n        let (prod, mut cons) = ring.split();\n        assert!(prod.user().iter().all(|w| w.load(Ordering::Relaxed) == 0));\n        // Both endpoints see the same line; a producer-side\n        // store is visible through the consumer's accessor.\n        prod.user()[0].store(7, Ordering::Release);\n        assert_eq!(cons.user()[0].load(Ordering::Acquire), 7);\n        // The user line must not alias ring state: scribbling\n        // all of it leaves the ring empty and functional.\n        for w in cons.user().iter() {\n            w.store(u32::MAX, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        }\n        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn indices_survive_u32_wrap() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let ring = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();\n        // Simulate a long-running ring two commits shy of the\n        // u32 wrap (empty state: p == c).\n        let start = u32::MAX - 1;\n        ring.header.producer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        ring.header.consumer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = ring.split();\n\n        // Fill: producer_idx runs MAX-1, MAX, 0, 1 — masked\n        // slot positions 2, 3, 0, 1.\n        for i in 0..4u64 {\n            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n            slot.seq = i;\n            slot.val = i;\n            slot.commit();\n        }\n        assert!(prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());\n\n        // Drain in order across the wrap.\n        for i in 0..4u64 {\n            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n            msg.release();\n        }\n        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn roundtrip_full_empty() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        // Empty at start.\n        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());\n\n        // Fill all 4 slots, then Full.\n        for i in 0..4u64 {\n            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n            slot.seq = i;\n            slot.val = i * 10;\n            slot.commit();\n        }\n        assert!(prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());\n\n        // Drain in order; wrap: slot 0 is reused by seq 4.\n        for i in 0..4u64 {\n            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n            assert_eq!(\n                *msg,\n                Msg {\n                    seq: i,\n                    val: i * 10\n                }\n            );\n            msg.release();\n        }\n        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());\n\n        // One more write lands in the masked-around slot 0.\n        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n        slot.seq = 4;\n        slot.commit();\n        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 4);\n        msg.release();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    // Dropping the guards is the behavior under test; they have\n    // no Drop impl by design (abandon = do nothing).\n    #[allow(clippy::drop_non_drop)]\n    fn abandoned_guards_publish_nothing() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        // Reserve then drop: nothing published.\n        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n        slot.seq = 99;\n        drop(slot);\n        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());\n\n        // Commit one, reserve then drop: still unread, the\n        // next reservation returns it again.\n        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n        slot.seq = 1;\n        slot.commit();\n        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);\n        drop(msg);\n        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);\n        msg.release();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn threaded_spsc() {\n        // Reduced under Miri: interpreted spin loops are slow,\n        // and its scheduler explores interleavings at any count.\n        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 200 } else { 100_000 };\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        std::thread::scope(|s| {\n            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT {\n                    loop {\n                        match prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false) {\n                            Ok(mut slot) => {\n                                slot.seq = i;\n                                slot.val = i * 3;\n                                slot.commit();\n                                break;\n                            }\n                            Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            });\n            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT {\n                    loop {\n                        match cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false) {\n                            Ok(msg) => {\n                                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                                assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);\n                                msg.release();\n                                break;\n                            }\n                            Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            });\n        });\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn reserve_slot_with_policy_counts_and_gives_up() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        // Empty ring: the consumer's policy sees 0-based\n        // attempts and its `false` becomes Err(Empty).\n        let mut seen = Vec::new();\n        let err = cons\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|attempt| {\n                seen.push(attempt);\n                attempt < 2\n            })\n            .err()\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(err, Empty);\n        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);\n\n        // Fill the ring: the producer's policy gives up with\n        // Err(Full).\n        for i in 0..4 {\n            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();\n            slot.seq = i;\n            slot.commit();\n        }\n        let err = prod\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|attempt| attempt < 2)\n            .err()\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(err, Full);\n\n        // With room / messages available the policy is never\n        // consulted.\n        let msg = cons\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| panic!(\"policy consulted with a message available\"))\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 0);\n        msg.release();\n        let mut slot = prod\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| panic!(\"policy consulted with room available\"))\n            .unwrap();\n        slot.seq = 4;\n        slot.commit();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn threaded_spsc_spin_variants() {\n        // The threaded_spsc flow through the shipped\n        // policy::spin on both ends — the shipped-policy model\n        // in use.\n        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 200 } else { 100_000 };\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        std::thread::scope(|s| {\n            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT {\n                    let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap();\n                    slot.seq = i;\n                    slot.val = i * 3;\n                    slot.commit();\n                }\n            });\n            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT {\n                    let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap();\n                    assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                    assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);\n                    msg.release();\n                }\n            });\n        });\n    }\n}\n"
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/lib.rs",
  "content": "//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided\n//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:\n//!\n//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`Header`] spanning four cache lines\n//!   (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index, app\n//!   user words), followed by M slots of N bytes.\n//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot\n//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.\n//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a\n//!   [`WriteSlot`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a\n//!   [`ReadSlot`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no\n//!   serialization step. Each side reserves at most one slot at\n//!   a time; the guard holds the endpoint borrow until commit /\n//!   release (or drop).\n//! - The SPSC protocol lives in the `spsc` module; a\n//!   multi-producer sibling, [`MpscRing`], lives in the\n//!   `mpsc` module (gated on CAS support) — see its module\n//!   docs for the claim/seq protocol and closure-send API.\n//!   Primitive modules hold versioned sibling implementations\n//!   (`spsc::v0`, …) behind per-module champion aliases; this\n//!   crate root re-exports the champions.\n//!\n//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md\n\n#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]\n\nuse core::mem::{align_of, size_of};\nuse core::sync::atomic::AtomicU32;\n\n// The MPSC ring needs CAS (the claim), so it is gated; the\n// SPSC ring protocol stays load/store-only. (The pool's\n// free-stack also uses CAS and predates the gate — see\n// notes/bugs.md.)\n#[cfg(target_has_atomic = \"32\")]\nmod mpsc;\npub mod policy;\nmod pool;\nmod registry;\npub mod spsc;\n\n#[cfg(target_has_atomic = \"32\")]\npub use mpsc::{MpscConsumer, MpscHeader, MpscProducer, MpscReadSlot, MpscRing, mpsc_region_size};\npub use pool::{BufSlot, Exhausted, Pool, PoolHeader, PoolResolver};\npub use registry::{Desc, PoolId, PoolRegistry, RegistryError};\npub use spsc::{Consumer, Header, Producer, ReadSlot, Ring, WriteSlot};\n\n/// Cache-line size the layout is built around.\n///\n/// - Slot size must be a multiple of this; slots and the region\n///   itself must be aligned to it.\npub const CACHE_LINE_SIZE: usize = 64;\n\n/// Number of `AtomicU32` words in the header's app-owned\n/// `user` line.\npub const USER_WORDS: usize = 16;\n\n/// `reserve_slot_with` / `send_with` failed: every slot holds\n/// an uncommitted-or-unread message.\n///\n/// - Shared by the ring primitives (SPSC reserve, MPSC send) —\n///   defined in the crate core so no primitive depends on a\n///   sibling's version module for its error type.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Full;\n\n/// `reserve_slot_with` failed: no unread messages.\n///\n/// - Shared by the ring primitives' consumer endpoints — see\n///   [`Full`] for why it lives in the crate core.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Empty;\n\n/// Cache-line-aligned wrapper granting its field sole\n/// ownership of the line.\n///\n/// - `repr(align(N))` accepts only an integer literal — it\n///   cannot name [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] — so the `64` is written out\n///   and a const assert ties them back together.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\nstruct CacheAligned<T>(T);\n\nconst _: () = assert!(align_of::<CacheAligned<AtomicU32>>() == CACHE_LINE_SIZE);\n\nimpl<T> core::ops::Deref for CacheAligned<T> {\n    type Target = T;\n    /// Access the wrapped value.\n    fn deref(&self) -> &T {\n        &self.0\n    }\n}\n\n/// Errors from region validation — [`Ring::init`] /\n/// [`Ring::attach`] and [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`].\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum Error {\n    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned.\n    Misaligned,\n    /// Region is smaller than header + slots/buffers.\n    TooSmall,\n    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.\n    BadSlotSize,\n    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.\n    BadCapacity,\n    /// Pool: buffer size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]\n    /// multiple.\n    BadBufSize,\n    /// Pool: buffer count is zero or `u32::MAX` (the\n    /// free-stack NIL sentinel).\n    BadBufCount,\n    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a region of the expected\n    /// kind.\n    BadMagic,\n    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.\n    BadLayoutVersion,\n    /// Attach: region built with a different [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`].\n    BadCacheLine,\n}\n\n/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per `reserve_slot_with`\n/// (both endpoints).\n///\n/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming\n///   error, not a runtime condition.\nfn check_type<T>(slot_size: u32) {\n    assert!(size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize, \"T larger than slot\");\n    assert!(\n        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE_SIZE,\n        \"T alignment exceeds slot alignment\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Non-panicking form of [`check_type`], for descriptor\n/// resolve — there the pool compared against is selected by\n/// untrusted input, which must not be able to select a panic.\nfn type_fits<T>(slot_size: u32) -> bool {\n    size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize && core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE_SIZE\n}\n\n/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.\n///\n/// - Returned range is `slot_size` bytes, cache-line aligned\n///   (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).\nfn slot_ptr(slots: *mut u8, idx: u32, mask: u32, slot_size: u32) -> *mut u8 {\n    let pos = (idx & mask) as usize;\n    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot\n    // array validated at init/attach.\n    unsafe { slots.add(pos * slot_size as usize) }\n}\n"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

LG, but if for some reason in mode.rs we can `pub mode v0,v1` and mix and match individual sub-modules, modulo type and impl fns are are used "appropriately". There will be no name collisions from the exporting PoV, correct?

> AGENT

Correct, with one precise boundary:

- **Declaring both** — `pub mod v0; pub mod v1;` can never collide. Every item lives under its own path (`spsc::v0::Ring` vs `spsc::v1::Ring` are simply different names); Rust namespaces by full path, so any number of versions coexist exporting identical item names.
- **The champion aliases are the only collision surface** — `pub use v0::Ring;` and `pub use v1::Ring;` together in `spsc/mod.rs` is a compile error (two things named `spsc::Ring`). So per *name*, the flat alias picks exactly one version — but it's per name, not per version: you *can* alias `v0::Ring` alongside `v1::SomeNewThing`, or expose both under renames (`pub use v1::Ring as SeamRing;`). And crucially, a mistake here fails loudly at compile time, never silently.
- **Your "modulo used appropriately" caveat is the real constraint** — mixed aliases must be type-coherent: `v0::Ring::split` returns `v0::Producer`, so aliasing `Ring` from v0 and `Producer` from v1 gives you handles that don't connect. The compiler catches that too (different types), so even misuse is a build error, not a runtime surprise.

Net: mix-and-match is safe-by-construction — the worst outcome is a compile error.

> DEVELOPER

So let's take a slightly different scenario, I want iiac-benches to implement benchmarks for v0 and v1
and what happens right now as iiac-benches is using so it's either not going to find it or use v0 which is it?

  use zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};

> AGENT

It finds it, and it gets **v0 — but only because the champion currently points there**. The resolution chain is:

```
zc_ring_x1::Consumer        (crate root re-export in lib.rs)
  → spsc::Consumer          (champion alias in spsc/mod.rs)
    → spsc::v0::Consumer    (today's champion)
```

So nothing breaks — that `use` keeps compiling forever. But it's important to be clear what it *means*: `zc_ring_x1::Consumer` is **"whatever the champion is,"** not "v0." Today those coincide. The day we flip the champion to v1, iiac-perf's existing benches silently start measuring v1 — same names, same code, different implementation. That's by design (the champion is the crate's public default), but for a benchmark it's a trap: results would change without the bench changing.

So for the v0-vs-v1 comparison scenario, iiac-perf should use **explicit pinned paths** for anything it wants to compare:

```rust
use zc_ring_x1::spsc::v0::{Consumer as ConsumerV0, Producer as ProducerV0};
use zc_ring_x1::spsc::v1::{Consumer as ConsumerV1, Producer as ProducerV1};
```

Both link into one bench binary, each bench line names exactly what it measures, and the champion flip can't silently move a data series. The root-path form stays legitimate for one thing only: a deliberate "current default" bench line that's *supposed* to track the champion.

One wrinkle to know about: `Full`/`Empty` just moved to the crate root as *shared* types — so `zc_ring_x1::Full` is version-independent (v0 and v1 both return the same `Full`). Match arms and error handling in benches work across versions unchanged.

Practical upshot for the existing `zcr-*` benches: they keep compiling and keep measuring v0 today with zero edits; when a v1 lands, we'd pin the existing lines to `spsc::v0::` explicitly so the series stays stable, and add `v1` lines beside them.
